Leave-one-out ablations confirm that each of the three prior pathways and both loss terms contribute on their own. In downstream detection, the synthetic data raises the group-pooled AP50 of rare classes by $1.23\times$ to $7.40\times$ over a real-data-only baseline. Code and pre-trained models are available at https://github.com/52hz-whale/TrafficSignInpaint.

</details>


### [69] [GlyphAnchor: Enhancing Visual Text Rendering via Position-Anchored Glyph Priors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02349)
*Qiang Xiang,Shuang Sun,Binglei Li,Yibo Chen,Xu Tang,Yao Hu,Junping Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GlyphAnchor is a novel method to enhance text rendering in diffusion transformer models for both text-to-image generation and image editing. It uses lightweight glyph patch conditions anchored to the target image through native positional encoding, trained with staged supervised finetuning and text-aware post-training. The method also introduces InfoTextBench, a new benchmark for evaluating text-rich visual text rendering.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing text rendering approaches in image generation models struggle with long, complex, densely arranged text and rare characters. Current methods either improve native rendering without explicit glyph priors or incorporate glyph priors but remain insufficiently accurate and robust in challenging scenarios.

Method: GlyphAnchor enhances diffusion transformer backbones with lightweight glyph patch conditions positioned through the model's native positional encoding. Training involves two stages: (1) staged supervised finetuning and (2) text-aware post-training for improved robustness. The method works for both text-to-image and image-editing models.

Result: Experiments across multiple backbones and benchmarks demonstrate that GlyphAnchor consistently improves text fidelity while preserving overall image quality, particularly in challenging scenarios including long, complex, densely arranged text and rare characters.

Conclusion: GlyphAnchor provides an effective solution for enhancing text rendering accuracy in diffusion transformer models. The introduction of InfoTextBench enables standardized evaluation of text-rich visual text rendering in both generation and editing settings.

Abstract: Rendering accurate text remains difficult for image generation and editing models, especially when the target contains long, complex, and densely arranged text or rare characters. Existing approaches either improve native text rendering through stronger backbones and data-centric training without explicit glyph priors, or incorporate glyph priors through specialized designs that remain insufficiently accurate and robust under challenging scenarios. We introduce GlyphAnchor, a novel text-rendering enhancement method for both text-to-image and image-editing diffusion transformer models. GlyphAnchor enhances the backbone with lightweight glyph patch conditions whose positions are anchored to the target image through the model's native positional encoding. We train this capability with staged supervised finetuning and further refine it with text-aware post-training to improve robustness. We also introduce InfoTextBench, a benchmark for evaluating text-rich visual text rendering in both generation and editing settings. Experiments across multiple backbones and benchmarks, including long, complex, and densely arranged text and rare character scenarios, show that GlyphAnchor consistently improves text fidelity while preserving overall image quality.

</details>


### [70] [LookStep: Efficient Vision-Language Navigation with Linguistic Foresight and Event Driven Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02350)
*Kun-Yang Yu,Yingzhe Li,Hongyu Xu,Shi-Yu Tian,Zhi Zhou,Yang Chen,Ming Yang,Sheng Wang,Qing Yu,Lan-Zhe Guo,Yu-Feng Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LookStep is a unified end-to-end framework for resource-efficient Vision-Language Navigation that uses Language Centric Future State Modeling and Event Driven Rolling Memory to achieve 49.7% success rate on R2R-CE Val-Unseen with better memory efficiency and less data usage compared to existing methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing VLN methods follow a next-step action prediction paradigm that only supervises expert actions, requiring large amounts of training data. They also rely on cognitive maps, accumulated historical frames, or external 3D tools, leading to high computational and memory overhead. The goal is to achieve resource-efficient VLN.

Method: LookStep combines two key components: 1) Language Centric Future State Modeling - uses language labels to generate coarse-grained navigation progress and future states for each candidate action; 2) Event Driven Rolling Memory - autonomously decides whether to write each observation into a bounded rolling memory with a semantic role.

Result: LookStep achieves 49.7% success rate on R2R-CE Val-Unseen, outperforming existing methods under the same training settings with better memory efficiency and less data usage.

Conclusion: The proposed LookStep framework successfully addresses the limitations of existing VLN methods by providing a unified end-to-end solution that is more data-efficient and memory-efficient while achieving superior navigation performance.

Abstract: Vision-Language Navigation (VLN) requires an embodied agent to follow natural-language instructions in unseen environments. Recent progress has been largely driven by Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). Existing methods follow a next-step action prediction paradigm, supervising only the expert action, which requires a high quantity of data for training. They also rely on cognitive maps, accumulated historical frames, or external 3D tools to maintain states, leading to high computational and memory overhead. To realize resource efficiency VLN, we propose LookStep, a unified end-to-end framework that combines Language Centric Future State Modeling and Event Driven Rolling Memory that uses language labels to generate coarse-grained navigation progress and future states for each candidate action, while autonomously deciding whether to write each observation into a bounded rolling memory with a semantic role. We validate LookStep empirically. On VLN-CE tasks, LookStep outperforms existing methods under the same training settings, achieving a 49.7\% success rate on R2R-CE Val-Unseen with better memory efficiency and less data usage. Code and model is available at https://github.com/kunyang-YU/LookStep.

</details>


### [71] [TempoGround: State-Aware Streaming Visual Grounding with Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02359)
*Leqian Ding,Junning Qiu,Manwen Yang,Yu Guo,Fei Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TempoGround is a VLM-native framework for visual grounding under streaming inputs that addresses identity drift, cross-frame inconsistency, and fragile localization through curriculum prediction and state-aware cross-frame correspondence, achieving significant improvements in 2D and 3D grounding metrics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing visual grounding methods under streaming inputs suffer from identity drift, cross-frame inconsistency, and fragile localization under partial occlusion, limiting their practical applicability for open-vocabulary perception with vision-language models.

Method: TempoGround uses a curriculum prediction mechanism with state-aware cross-frame correspondence to resolve 2D instance association, predict object presence states (enter, continue, leave), decode 2D boxes, and lift them to 3D camera-frame boxes. It incorporates Streaming Grounding Reinforcement (SGR) with verifiable Grounding, Identity, and Consistency rewards, trained via a three-stage strategy on large-scale data.

Result: TempoGround improves F1_2D@0.5 by 4.4 and F1_2D@0.95 by 0.5 on average, and F1_3D@0.25 by 6.2 and AP_3D by 7.5, respectively.

Conclusion: TempoGround provides a practical foundation for visual grounding under streaming inputs, demonstrating significant improvements in both 2D and 3D grounding tasks across multiple challenging benchmarks.

Abstract: Visual grounding maps language referents to spatial targets and is central to open-vocabulary perception with vision-language models. Existing methods have made substantial progress on single-frame and video-based visual grounding, yet under streaming inputs they still suffer from identity drift, cross-frame inconsistency, and fragile localization under partial occlusion. To address these issues, we present TempoGround, a VLM-native framework that detects cross-frame object correspondence and explicitly models object presence states, thereby enabling accurate and consistent visual grounding under streaming inputs. The key is a curriculum prediction mechanism guided by state-aware cross-frame correspondence: TempoGround resolves 2D instance association, predicts whether each object newly enters, continues in, or leaves the view, decodes the 2D box, and then lifts it to a camera-frame 3D box. As token-level supervision alone cannot capture the geometric objectives of streaming grounding, we further introduce Streaming Grounding Reinforcement (SGR), which optimizes TempoGround with verifiable Grounding, Identity, and Consistency rewards, jointly reinforcing persistent localization and temporally consistent predictions. We carefully design a three-stage training strategy and train TempoGround on large-scale data. We evaluate visual grounding under causally streaming inputs on multiple challenging benchmarks: TempoGround improves F1_2D@0.5 and F1_2D@0.95 by 4.4 and 0.5 on average, and F1_3D@0.25 and AP_3D by 6.2 and 7.5, respectively. These results demonstrate that TempoGround provides a practical foundation for visual grounding under streaming inputs.

</details>


### [72] [Information Density Imbalance in Visual Object Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02369)
*Ziwei Zhao,Yanxi Lu,Yuwei Hu,Shiyang Su,Mingxuan Wang,Chenyue Zhou,Jiayi Chen,Hehan Li,Xiaoshuai Hao,Andi Zhang,Yanbiao Ma*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper challenges the conventional assumption that instance count alone determines category bias in object detection datasets. The authors introduce "information density" as a new metric to measure category bias, demonstrating that even datasets with balanced instance numbers exhibit bias. By incorporating information density into three advanced loss functions, they achieve reduced model bias and improved overall performance on Pascal VOC, COCO-LT, and LVIS datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The traditional approach to understanding category bias in object detection relies on instance count, assuming fewer instances lead to poor model performance. However, this assumption fails to explain why bias persists even in datasets with relatively balanced instance numbers. This gap motivated the authors to investigate alternative sources of category bias beyond instance count.

Method: The authors first introduce the concept and measurement of information density. They then investigate the relationship between information density and model accuracy, examining how training processes impact this relationship. Based on empirical findings, they propose simple improvements to three advanced object detection loss functions by incorporating information density.

Result: The study reveals a significant negative correlation between a category's information density and its accuracy. Experiments on Pascal VOC, COCO-LT, and LVIS datasets demonstrate that information density-based improvements can significantly reduce model bias while effectively enhancing overall performance of existing loss functions.

Conclusion: Information density imbalance may be a potential source of category bias in object detection. This research provides a new perspective for understanding generalized bias phenomena and offers new tools for designing fairer loss functions and training strategies. The simple improvements to existing loss functions show promising results in reducing bias while improving performance.

Abstract: In object detection, the number of instances is typically used to determine whether a dataset exhibits a long-tailed distribution, implicitly assuming that the model will perform poorly on categories with fewer instances. This assumption has led to extensive research on category bias in datasets with imbalanced instance numbers. However, even in datasets where instance numbers are relatively balanced, models still exhibit category bias, indicating that instance count alone cannot explain this phenomenon. In this work, we first introduce the concept and measurement of information density. We then observe a significant negative correlation between a category's information density and its accuracy, and we investigate how the training process impacts this relationship. Empirical studies suggest that information density imbalance may be a potential source of category bias. To preliminarily validate the potential of information density, we made simple improvements to three advanced object detection loss functions using this concept. Experiments on the Pascal VOC, COCO-LT, and LVIS datasets demonstrate that information density can significantly reduce model bias while effectively enhancing the overall performance of existing loss functions. This study provides a new perspective for understanding the generalized bias phenomenon in object detection models and offers new tools for designing fairer loss functions and training strategies.

</details>


### [73] [ProSR: Semantic-Prototype-Guided Discrete Modeling for Physically Consistent SAR Super-Resolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02377)
*Byoungwoo Kim,Munchurl Kim*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ProSR is a novel Semantic Prototype-Guided Super-Resolution framework for SAR imagery that reformulates image super-resolution as a semantically-guided discrete token prediction task within a quantized latent space, preserving scattering statistics while achieving superior visual quality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current smooth-approximation based diffusion frameworks struggle to preserve coherent scattering statistics in SAR imagery, causing stochastic structural distortions inconsistent with real SAR physics. Additionally, label scarcity in SAR datasets limits the use of supervised learning approaches.

Method: ProSR uses: (1) Self-Supervised Learning backbone to extract label-free semantic priors; (2) Semantic-Aligned Detail Encoding to decouple high-frequency signals into discrete scattering primitives; (3) Semantic Prototype Map Generator to construct semantic prototype maps; (4) Prototype-Map-Guided Attention to route information flows within identical categories and mitigate inter-class interference.

Result: ProSR achieves superior visual quality while preserving essential scattering characteristics required for practical SAR applications, validated on a large-scale 0.25m resolution benchmark from the Umbra Open Dataset.

Conclusion: The proposed ProSR framework successfully addresses the challenge of preserving SAR scattering statistics during super-resolution by introducing semantic prototype guidance and discrete token prediction, offering a promising solution for practical SAR applications requiring high-resolution imagery.

Abstract: High-resolution Synthetic Aperture Radar (SAR) imagery is critical for precision analysis such as automatic target recognition, yet its acquisition is costly. Although generative image super-resolution (ISR) models offer a promising alternative, current smooth-approximation based diffusion frameworks often struggle to preserve the coherent scattering statistics, causing stochastic structural distortions that are less consistent with real SAR physics. To address this, we propose Semantic Prototype-Guided Super-Resolution (ProSR), reformulating SAR ISR as a semantically-guided discrete token prediction task within a quantized latent space. By mapping signal features to discrete scattering primitives, ProSR preserves the impulsive nature of SAR without over-smoothing. Furthermore, we integrate a Self-Supervised Learning backbone into SAR ISR to extract label-free semantic priors, overcoming label scarcity. Guided by these priors, we introduce Semantic-Aligned Detail Encoding to decouple high-frequency signals into discrete scattering primitives. In parallel, the Semantic Prototype Map Generator explicitly constructs semantic prototype maps, allowing Prototype-Map-Guided Attention to route the information flows within identical categories and mitigate inter-class interference. To validate our approach, we present a large-scale 0.25m resolution benchmark from the Umbra Open Dataset. Experimental results show ProSR achieves superior visual quality while preserving essential scattering characteristics required for practical SAR applications.

</details>


### [74] [CA-OPD: Confidence-Aware On-Policy Distillation for Structured Visual Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02401)
*Menghao Li,Linjie Mu,Yin Wang,Haotian Hu,Yannian Gu,Lujiayi Xue,Fanyi Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: CA-OPD introduces confidence-aware on-policy distillation for autoregressive vision language models, using teacher confidence to selectively correct unreliable student transitions and aligning knowledge transfer with intervention decisions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Autoregressive vision language models suffer from compounding errors. While on-policy distillation (OPD) addresses training-inference mismatch, unreliable student predictions (especially early in training) can derail training. Existing interleaved distillation methods rely on rigid ranking metrics rather than exact teacher confidence and overlook how intervention decisions inform token-level supervision.

Method: CA-OPD utilizes teacher confidence to selectively correct unreliable student transitions, gradually transferring rollout control to the student via a strict-to-relaxed schedule. Corrected positions receive direct cross-entropy supervision from the teacher's prediction, while retained positions benefit from the teacher's full predictive distribution. Evaluated in a multi-teacher setting for GUI grounding and OCR.

Result: CA-OPD substantially improves Qwen3.5-0.8B baseline across all six target benchmarks, including gains of 9.50 points on ScreenSpot-Pro and 6.72 points on OCRBench-v2 English.

Conclusion: The gains depend on intervention placement, progressive rollout control, and intervention-aligned supervision, rather than intervention frequency alone. This demonstrates the importance of confidence-aware intervention and adaptive supervision in on-policy distillation.

Abstract: Autoregressive vision language models unify heterogeneous perception tasks but are highly susceptible to compounding errors. On-policy distillation (OPD) bridges the training-inference mismatch by training students on their own rollouts. However, unreliable student predictions, especially early in training, can derail the trajectory and degrade the quality of teacher supervision. While recent interleaved distillation methods allow the teacher to verify and replace student tokens, they primarily rely on rigid ranking metrics rather than exact teacher confidence, and they overlook how intervention decisions can inform token-level supervision. To address this, we introduce Confidence-Aware On-Policy Distillation (CA-OPD), a framework that couples reliable rollout construction with adaptive supervision. CA-OPD utilizes teacher confidence to selectively correct unreliable student transitions, gradually transferring rollout control to the student via a strict-to-relaxed schedule. Crucially, CA-OPD aligns knowledge transfer with these intervention decisions: corrected positions receive direct cross-entropy supervision from the teacher's prediction, while retained positions benefit from the teacher's full predictive distribution. Evaluated in a multi-teacher setting for GUI grounding and optical character recognition, CA-OPD substantially improves the Qwen3.5-0.8B baseline across all six target benchmarks, including gains of $9.50$ points on ScreenSpot-Pro and $6.72$ points on OCRBench-v2 English. Controlled studies further show that the gains depend on intervention placement, progressive rollout control, and intervention-aligned supervision, rather than intervention frequency alone.

</details>


### [75] [The Diagnosis a Reporter Leaves Unspoken: Surfacing Frozen Tumor Features for Brain-Tumor MRI Reporting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02411)
*Khawaja Murad ul Hassan,Ruqiyya Adil,Adil Qayyum,Rida Hassan,Asad Mansoor Khan,Muhammad Usman Akram,Mehran Ebrahimi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: NeuroFusion is an assistive brain-MRI report generator that extracts latent diagnostic signals from a frozen segmentation model using discriminative classifier heads, then conditions a draft-then-review decoder to produce accurate diagnoses. It significantly outperforms the baseline model in diagnosis accuracy (meningioma 0.92, metastasis 0.75 vs nearly zero recall), prose quality (8/9 comparisons), and latency (5-6x faster), while maintaining schema validity and reducing contradictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The core problem is that a capable brain-MRI report generator (based on medical Mistral-7B) fails to produce accurate diagnoses despite having latent knowledge - it misclassifies most meningiomas and metastases as gliomas. The authors discovered that a linear probe on the model's segmentation features could recover tumor classification at 0.82 macro-F1, suggesting the diagnostic information exists but isn't properly expressed in the generated reports.

Method: NeuroFusion uses discriminative field-classifier heads applied to per-lesion features extracted from a frozen segmentation model. These classifier heads condition a fast, single-pass draft-then-review decoder on their outputs. The key design principle is to "surface" the latent diagnostic signal rather than override it. Grammar-constrained decoding ensures schema validity. A controlled experiment tested a "learned diagnosis pin" that overrides the decoder instead of merely informing it.

Result: NeuroFusion achieves diagnosis recall of 0.92 for meningioma and 0.75 for metastasis (vs 0.44/0.07 for baseline). It wins 8 of 9 prose-content comparisons across three held-out cohorts using RaTEScore, RadGraph-F1, and GREEN metrics (Holm-corrected paired BCa). Latency is reduced 5-6x (~80 vs 457 seconds per case). Grammar-constrained decoding achieves 92.3% schema validity with only 7.5% contradicted sentences vs 36.8% for baseline. The negative control (diagnosis pin overriding decoder) collapses out-of-distribution metastasis recall to 0.03. In a blinded nine-case pilot, two neurologists rated NeuroFusion highest in every tumor type, the only system with zero critical errors, and gave top sign-off in 8/9 cases.

Conclusion: The key insight is that assistive reporting systems should surface latent diagnostic signals rather than override them. Conditioning the decoder on classifier outputs (rather than forcing specific diagnoses) preserves the model's ability to handle out-of-distribution cases. NeuroFusion demonstrates that combining frozen segmentation features with discriminative classifier heads and a draft-then-review decoder can produce accurate, low-latency, and reliable clinical reports, as validated by board-certified neurologists in a blinded pilot study.

Abstract: A capable brain-MRI report generator can still be, in effect, diagnostically silent. When a multi-chain chain-of-thought (CoT) reporter built on a medical Mistral-7B backbone is evaluated on held-out cohorts, it names most meningiomas and almost all metastases "glioma" (diagnosis recall 0.44/0.07). Yet the answer is not absent from the model: a supervised linear probe applied to its frozen segmentation features recovers the three tumour cohorts at 0.82 macro-F$_1$ (5-fold cross-validation; chance $\approx$0.33). We introduce NeuroFusion, an assistive reporter that surfaces this latent signal rather than overriding it: discriminative field-classifier heads over per-lesion features condition a fast, single-pass draft-then-review decoder on their committed outputs. Built on the identical Mistral backbone, this restores the diagnosis (meningioma 0.92, metastasis 0.75) and wins 8 of 9 prose-content comparisons across three held-out cohorts (RaTEScore, RadGraph-F$_1$, GREEN; Holm-corrected paired BCa), with no significant loss on the ninth, at 5-6x lower latency ($\approx$80 vs. 457 s/case). A controlled negative result sharpens the mechanism: a learned diagnosis pin that overrides the decoder instead of merely informing it collapses out-of-distribution metastasis recall to 0.03. Grammar-constrained decoding keeps 92.3% of records schema-valid, making every sentence entailment-checkable (7.5% contradicted vs. 36.8% for the direct baseline). In a blinded nine-case pilot, two board-certified neurologists independently rated NeuroFusion highest in every tumour type, the only system with zero critical errors, and gave it the top-rated sign-off in eight of nine cases (six outright, two ties).

</details>


### [76] [Uncertainty-Guided Adverse Weather Restoration via Gated Transformer Network](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02434)
*Zheke Jin,Yuning Cui,Tianle Jin,Alois Knoll,Hu Cao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: UAR-Net is a novel weather-specific all-in-one adverse weather image restoration network that addresses spatially heterogeneous degradations through gated transformers, balanced multi-scale skip connections, and uncertainty-aware refinement, achieving state-of-the-art performance across multiple benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing weather-specific restoration models suffer from three key limitations: (1) weather-agnostic global aggregation that ignores heterogeneous degradations, (2) naive cross-scale fusion that fails to effectively integrate multi-scale features, and (3) deterministic objectives that lack uncertainty estimation. These issues are particularly problematic in all-in-one (AiO) adverse-weather settings where degradations are spatially heterogeneous.

Method: The proposed UAR-Net integrates three key components: (1) Gated Dual-scale Transformer Blocks (GDTB) that jointly model selective global interactions and multi-scale local structures; (2) Progressive Balanced Multi-scale Skip Connection (BMSC) for balanced multi-scale feature integration; (3) Uncertainty-Aware Refinement Head (URH) that performs artifact removal, detail enhancement, and predictive uncertainty estimation. The model is supervised by a Brightness-Aware Energy Loss (BAE-Loss) to encourage accurate reconstruction with well-calibrated uncertainty.

Result: The method achieves state-of-the-art performance across multiple adverse-weather benchmarks. The codes will be open-sourced upon acceptance.

Conclusion: UAR-Net effectively addresses the challenge of spatially heterogeneous degradations in all-in-one adverse weather image restoration through its novel architecture combining gated transformers, balanced multi-scale fusion, and uncertainty-aware refinement, demonstrating superior performance over existing methods.

Abstract: Restoring images degraded by adverse weather remains challenging due to spatially heterogeneous degradations. Many existing weather-specific restoration models rely on weather-agnostic global aggregation, naive cross-scale fusion, and deterministic objectives, which struggle to handle heterogeneous degradations in all-in-one adverse-weather settings. To address these limitations, we propose an Uncertainty-guided Adverse-weather Restoration Network (UAR-Net), a weather-specific AiO framework that integrates a gated transformer with balanced multi-scale skip connections. Specifically, we employ Gated Dual-scale Transformer Blocks (GDTB) to jointly model selective global interactions and multi-scale local structures, a progressive Balanced Multi-scale Skip Connection (BMSC) for balanced multi-scale feature integration, and an Uncertainty-Aware Refinement Head (URH) that performs artifact removal, detail enhancement, and predictive uncertainty estimation. The model is supervised by a Brightness-Aware Energy Loss (BAE-Loss) to encourage accurate reconstruction with well-calibrated uncertainty. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method achieves state-of-the-art performance across multiple adverse-weather benchmarks. The codes will open source upon acceptance.

</details>


### [77] [Adapting a Foundation Model for Lunar Surface Height Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02448)
*Patrick Bauer,Marius Schwinning,Melanie Siegel,Andreas Weinmann,Hichem Snoussi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a fine-tuning strategy for the Depth Anything V2 (DAV2) model to estimate relative lunar surface height from monocular images, addressing the limitation of traditional hazard detection methods that rely only on 2D image data. The fine-tuned model significantly outperforms the zero-shot version.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The European Space Agency (ESA) requires precise height estimation for safe lunar landings, especially for hazardous terrain detection. While traditional methods like shape from shading (SfS) and stereophotogrammetry (SPG) exist, deep learning-based monocular depth estimation has become the focus. However, existing models lack adaptation to the lunar domain, leading to underperformance when applied to lunar surface height estimation.

Method: The methodology involves fine-tuning the pre-trained zero-shot relative depth estimation model Depth Anything V2 (DAV2) using publicly available SPG-derived DEM data of the lunar surface. This domain adaptation approach transforms the general-purpose DAV2 into a lunar-specific relative depth estimator.

Result: The fine-tuned model demonstrates significant performance improvement compared to the original zero-shot DAV2 model, effectively converting it into a reliable relative depth estimator for lunar surface analysis.

Conclusion: Fine-tuning a pre-trained monocular depth estimation model with lunar-specific DEM data is an effective approach to obtain accurate relative height information for lunar surface hazard detection. This method provides valuable additional information for hazard localization beyond traditional 2D image-based detection.

Abstract: Digital elevation models (DEMs) can provide accurate height information, making it invaluable for analyzing the lunar surface. As the European Space Agency (ESA) prepares for future lunar missions that aim to land on the Moon, a precise method for height estimation will be essential for hazardous terrain that could endanger the landing approach. Traditional approaches to generate DEMs from imagery, such as shape from shading (SfS) and stereophotogrammetry (SPG) have been proven highly valuable for this task. However, due to advancements in machine learning, especially computer vision, the focus has shifted towards monocular depth estimation via deep learning. The lunar surface is covered by rocks and craters, and classic hazard detection methods rely solely on 2D image data. Our goal is to address this issue by developing a relative lunar surface height estimator that can provide additional information for hazard localization. In this letter, we present a methodology that builds on the well-known zero-shot relative depth estimation model Depth Anything V2 (DAV2). Other works have been using it as a state-of-the-art comparison for their proposed lunar DEM estimation method, but without adaptations to the target domain. Thus, it may underperform. Therefore, we propose a fine-tuning strategy with publicly available SPG-derived DEM data of the lunar surface. Our results demonstrate a significant improvement in performance compared to the zero-shot model, effectively transforming DAV2 into a reliable relative depth estimator of the lunar surface.

</details>


### [78] [WiFlow: Estimating Optical Flow using WiFi Channel State Information](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02452)
*Thomas Weigel,Simon Kiefhaber,Fabian Portner,Matthias Hollick,Simone Schaub-Meyer*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: WiFlow proposes using WiFi channel state information (CSI) instead of cameras for optical flow estimation, addressing privacy concerns and lighting limitations of vision-based methods. The work includes a preprocessor evaluation, three model architectures with different trade-offs, and the first CSI-based optical flow dataset.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional camera-based optical flow estimation faces two key limitations: (1) privacy concerns when deploying cameras, and (2) degraded quality under poor lighting conditions. This work explores WiFi CSI as an alternative sensing modality that is privacy-preserving and lighting-independent.

Method: The paper proposes WiFlow, a CSI-based optical flow estimator consisting of: (1) a preprocessor evaluation for CSI data, and (2) three model architectures offering different accuracy-complexity trade-offs. A custom dataset was created for training and evaluation.

Result: The experiments provide insights into key design elements for CSI-based optical flow estimation, demonstrating the feasibility of using WiFi signals for motion estimation.

Conclusion: WiFi CSI can serve as a viable alternative to cameras for optical flow estimation, offering privacy preservation and robustness to lighting conditions. The introduced dataset and model architectures provide a foundation for future research in this domain.

Abstract: Knowing where and how fast objects are moving within a scene is important across various domains. Usually, cameras are used to capture the data necessary for this task, but adding cameras often raises privacy concerns, and the quality of captured frames is heavily influenced by lighting conditions. In this work, we explore using WiFi channel state information (CSI) instead of camera frames for optical flow estimation. We propose WiFlow, a CSI based flow estimator, a preprocessor evaluation for CSI, and three model architectures that offer different trade-offs between accuracy and complexity. Further, we create the first dataset for training and evaluating CSI-based optical flow estimators, and our experiments provide insights into key design elements for this task. Code and data are available at https://visinf.github.io/wiflow.

</details>


### [79] [VIPS: Vehicle-Infrastructure Cooperative Planning Benchmark via Pseudo-Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02462)
*Hoonhee Cho,Jae-Young Kang,Giwon Lee,Hyemin Yang,Heejun Park,Kuk-Jin Yoon*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: VIPS is a benchmark for cooperative autonomous driving in Vehicle-to-Infrastructure (V2I) settings that uses pseudo-simulation to enable scalable yet realistic evaluation without full simulation. The paper also proposes CoS-V2X, a cooperative planning framework using sparse representations for efficient communication and robust decision-making under heterogeneous observations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: End-to-end autonomous driving in urban environments requires robust decision-making under partial observability and complex multi-agent interactions. Severe occlusions and dense traffic at intersections limit the perception capability of single-agent systems. Additionally, existing evaluation protocols face a trade-off between open-loop (fails to capture error accumulation) and closed-loop (costly, difficult to scale, domain gaps) evaluation.

Method: VIPS extends pseudo-simulation by integrating vehicle and infrastructure observations to enable scalable yet realistic evaluation of robustness and error propagation. CoS-V2X models vehicle-infrastructure interactions using compact features (sparse representations) for efficient communication and robust decision-making under heterogeneous observations.

Result: The proposed approach enables scalable evaluation of cooperative autonomous driving without full simulation, while the sparse representation framework allows efficient communication between vehicle and infrastructure for robust decision-making.

Conclusion: The combination of VIPS benchmark and CoS-V2X framework provides a practical solution for evaluating and implementing V2I cooperative autonomous driving, addressing the limitations of both open-loop and closed-loop evaluation protocols while enabling efficient communication through sparse representations.

Abstract: End-to-end autonomous driving in urban environments requires robust decision-making under partial observability and complex multi-agent interactions. Severe occlusions and dense traffic at intersections limit the perception capability of single-agent systems, motivating recent efforts on Vehicle-to-Infrastructure (V2I) cooperation for perception and planning. However, existing evaluation protocols face a fundamental trade-off: open-loop evaluation fails to capture error accumulation and recovery from deviations, while closed-loop evaluation is costly, difficult to scale, and often relies on simulated environments that may suffer from domain gaps. To bridge this gap, we propose VIPS, a benchmark for cooperative autonomous driving in V2I settings based on pseudo-simulation. VIPS extends pseudo-simulation by integrating vehicle and infrastructure observations. This enables scalable yet realistic evaluation of robustness and error propagation without full simulation. We further present CoS-V2X, a cooperative planning framework based on sparse representations. CoS-V2X models vehicle-infrastructure interactions using compact features for efficient communication and robust decision-making under heterogeneous observations. Code and dataset are available at https://vips2026.github.io.

</details>


### [80] [Learning to Track from Privileged Target Appearances](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02471)
*Xin Chen,Jiao Xu,Dong Wang,Huchuan Lu,Kede Ma*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper addresses a key bottleneck in visual tracking where trackers must trade off between accurate but stale initial templates and fresh but uncertain recent templates. The authors propose PATT (Privileged Appearance Transfer for Tracking), a teacher-student framework that uses frame-level ground truths during training to transfer privileged appearance information to a deployable tracker through multi-level representation prediction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Visual trackers rely on target templates that suffer from a fundamental trade-off: initial ground-truth templates are exact but become stale over time, while recent templates better reflect current appearance but are cropped from uncertain predictions. The authors quantified this bottleneck with a non-deployable oracle supplying exact current-frame target crops, which improved AUC on LaSOT by 15.2 percentage points, revealing a significant training-only opportunity.

Method: The authors introduce PATT, a teacher-student training framework. The privileged teacher observes exact target crops from past, current, and future frames, while the student receives only past-frame templates and learns to predict the teacher's search representations. To avoid transferring unreliable teacher signals, PATT weights the transfer by the teacher's relative localization advantage over the student and its absolute localization accuracy. After training, all privileged components are removed for standard student-only inference.

Result: PATT achieves consistent gains across seven benchmarks at two model scales under both long- and short-term tracking protocols. The oracle experiments show a 15.2 percentage point AUC improvement on LaSOT, demonstrating the significant potential of privileged appearance information.

Conclusion: The paper demonstrates that frame-level ground truths during training can be leveraged to transfer privileged appearance information to a deployable tracker through multi-level representation prediction. The proposed PATT framework successfully bridges the gap between training (where exact crops are available) and deployment (where they are not), achieving consistent improvements across multiple benchmarks and model scales.

Abstract: Target templates define what a visual tracker searches for, yet the templates available at inference trade off localization certainty with appearance freshness: the initial ground-truth template is exact but becomes stale, whereas recent templates better reflect the current appearance but are cropped from uncertain predictions. We quantify this bottleneck with a non-deployable oracle that supplies an exact current-frame target crop, improving AUC on LaSOT by 15.2 percentage points. This gap reveals a training-only opportunity: frame-level ground truths provide exact current- and future-frame target crops, although such crops are unavailable at deployment. We introduce Privileged Appearance Transfer for Tracking (PATT), a teacher-student training framework that transfers these privileged appearances to a deployable tracker through multi-level representation prediction. The privileged teacher observes exact target crops from past, current, and future frames, whereas the student receives only past-frame templates and learns to predict the teacher's search representations. To avoid transferring unreliable teacher signals, PATT weights this transfer by the teacher's relative localization advantage over the student and its absolute localization accuracy. After training, the teacher, latent predictor, reliability weights, and privileged crops are removed, leaving standard student-only inference. Across seven benchmarks at two model scales, PATT achieves consistent gains under both long- and short-term tracking protocols.

</details>


### [81] [UnCapsTSR: An Unsupervised Transformer-based Image Super-Resolution Approach for Capsule Endoscopy Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02476)
*Anjali Sarvaiya,Shubh Kawa,Lalit Agrawal,Jagrit Joshi,Kishor Upla,Kiran Raja*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: UnCapsTSR is an unsupervised transformer-based GAN framework for super-resolving low-resolution Wireless Capsule Endoscopy images without requiring explicit degradation estimation or LR-HR pairs. It uses Bilateral Total Variation loss and introduces a new dataset and evaluation metric.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: WCE captures video through the GI tract but suffers from coarser resolution due to capsule size and wireless transmission limitations. Existing supervised SR methods require LR-HR pairs, which are difficult to obtain in medical imaging. This work aims to perform unsupervised super-resolution without explicit degradation estimation of real-world LR data.

Method: UnCapsTSR employs an unsupervised transformer-based GAN architecture with Bilateral Total Variation (BTV) loss to ensure spatial continuity in SR images. A new dataset curated from Kvasir Capsule is used for training, while generalization is validated on KID and GIANA datasets. A new non-reference metric (EndoQM) is introduced for domain-specific evaluation.

Result: Experiments demonstrate consistent improvement over state-of-the-art unsupervised SR approaches using NIQE, BRISQUE, PIQE, and EndoQM metrics. Statistical evaluation shows 40 to 80 percent improvement in EndoQM from LR to SR across evaluated datasets.

Conclusion: UnCapsTSR effectively improves WCE image resolution in an unsupervised manner, achieving significant quality improvements (40-80% in EndoQM) while generalizing well across different datasets not used during training, offering a practical solution for clinical WCE image enhancement.

Abstract: Wireless Capsule Endoscopy (WCE) captures and streams video while passing through a patient's Gastrointestinal (GI) tract and is used to examine its irregularities. Although advantageous over conventional endoscopy, WCE suffers from limitations related to capsule size and wireless transmission, resulting in images with coarser resolution. This work presents UnCapsTSR, an unsupervised transformer-based Generative Adversarial Network (GAN) framework for improving the spatial resolution of Low-Resolution (LR) WCE images. The proposed method accomplishes SR without explicit degradation estimation of real-world LR data and eliminates the need for true LR-HR pairs. UnCapsTSR employs a Bilateral Total Variation (BTV) loss to ensure spatial continuity in SR images. A newly curated dataset from the Kvasir Capsule dataset is also presented for training WCE SR models. Generalizability is validated on KID and GIANA datasets that are not used during training. A new non-reference metric, Endoscopy Quality Metric (EndoQM), is introduced for quantitative evaluation of domain-specific WCE data. Experiments demonstrate consistent improvement over state-of-the-art unsupervised SR approaches using NIQE, BRISQUE, PIQE, and EndoQM. Statistical evaluation shows 40 to 80 percent improvement in EndoQM from LR to SR across the evaluated datasets.

</details>


### [82] [Blending Concepts: Benchmarking Visual Metaphor Generation in Text-to-Image Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02502)
*Chuer Chen,Zichen Wang,Yi He,Zhengxi Yu,Nan Cao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: VMetaphor-Bench is the first benchmark for evaluating visual metaphor generation in text-to-image models, comprising 1,500 visual metaphors organized into three levels and ten categories with a hybrid MLLM-as-judge evaluation framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Text-to-image models excel at rendering specified objects and attributes but struggle with visual metaphors (images conveying abstract ideas by combining elements from two distinct domains), which remains an unexamined capability.

Method: The benchmark includes 1,500 visual metaphors from real-world creative imagery organized into three levels and ten categories, each with two prompts of varying specificity. Evaluation uses a hybrid MLLM-as-judge framework combining: (1) a multiple-choice question protocol with 9,594 questions across four levels of metaphorical fidelity, and (2) a dimension-based scoring protocol along three perceptual dimensions. Eleven representative T2I models were evaluated.

Result: Even the strongest proprietary models struggle with compositional structuring and cross-domain mapping, which are key aspects of metaphorical expression.

Conclusion: Visual metaphor generation represents an important frontier for future T2I research, as current models fail to adequately handle the compositional and cross-domain requirements of metaphorical imagery.

Abstract: Text-to-image (T2I) models have achieved remarkable success at faithfully rendering specified objects and attributes, yet their ability to produce visual metaphors, images that convey abstract ideas by combining elements from two distinct domains, remains largely unexamined. To bridge this gap, we introduce VMetaphor-Bench, the first benchmark for evaluating visual metaphor generation in T2I models. It comprises 1,500 visual metaphors curated from real-world creative imagery, organized into three levels and ten categories, with each sample paired with two prompts of differing specificity. For evaluation, we develop a hybrid framework within an MLLM-as-judge paradigm, combining a multiple-choice question (MCQ) based protocol of 9,594 questions across four levels of metaphorical fidelity with a dimension-based scoring protocol along three perceptual dimensions. Extensive evaluation of 11 representative T2I models reveals that even the strongest proprietary models struggle with compositional structuring and cross-domain mapping, key aspects of metaphorical expression, highlighting visual metaphor generation as an important frontier for future T2I research.

</details>


### [83] [SR-Edit: Region-Aware Image Editing via Self-Refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02504)
*Andong Wang,Zehua Chen,Yuxuan Jiang,Jun Zhu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SR-Edit is an image editing framework that uses iterative self-refinement to achieve faithful edits. It addresses the challenge of precisely modifying target regions while preserving non-edit areas by automatically inferring regions and enforcing consistency without external annotations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current image editing methods suffer from inaccurate region estimation and heuristic correction strategies that distort the model's native inference process, creating new artifacts. Existing approaches require external region annotations which are often difficult to obtain in practice.

Method: SR-Edit uses iterative self-refinement with two key steps per iteration: (1) Extract progressively precise and self-consistent region separation from the model's own predictions via lightweight post-processing, and (2) Enforce preservation in non-edit areas through correction updates that remain aligned with the original sampling dynamics.

Result: Extensive experiments demonstrate that SR-Edit achieves superior preservation and overall image quality compared to existing editing techniques.

Conclusion: The iterative self-refinement approach effectively addresses the limitations of prior methods by using the model's own predictions for region separation and maintaining alignment with original sampling dynamics, leading to improved fidelity in image editing.

Abstract: With the recent rapid progress in generative models, image editing has made remarkable advances, yet achieving faithful edits that precisely modify only the target regions while strictly preserving all other regions remains challenging. Since externally provided region annotations are often difficult to obtain in practice, a growing body of work seeks to improve preservation by automatically inferring edit and non-edit regions, and then enforcing consistency on the latter. However, these approaches still suffer from inaccurate region estimation and heuristic correction strategies that distort the native inference process, making methods designed for fidelity themselves a new source of artifacts. We propose SR-Edit, an image editing framework that overcomes these issues via iterative self-refinement. Specifically, at each iteration, SR-Edit first (i) extracts progressively precise and self-consistent region separation from the model's own predictions by lightweight post-processing, and then (ii) enforces preservation in non-edit areas through correction updates that remain aligned with the original sampling dynamics. Extensive experiments demonstrate that SR-Edit achieves superior preservation and overall image quality compared to existing editing techniques.

</details>


### [84] [Orthogonal Ensembles and Tested Explanations for Performer-Independent Body-Motion Emotion Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02510)
*Naoto Nishida,Yoshio Ishiguro*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper addresses 12-class acted-emotion classification from skeleton motion using leave-performer-out (LPO) evaluation. The key finding is that an ensemble of 11 models achieves 36.80% Macro-F1 (43% relative improvement over baseline STGCN++ at 25.73%). The main contribution is an explanation suite (part-masking, counterfactual edits) demonstrating that model decisions rely on motion-grounded body-region evidence that aligns with Laban Movement Analysis (LMA) attributes (Spearman rho = +0.500) rather than classical kinematics (rho = +0.033), a ~15x difference.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is to address a challenging underdetermined setting (12-class emotion classification from skeleton motion with LPO evaluation, where chance is only 8.3%). The authors note that reliable gains come not from new architectures but from combining models with orthogonal error modes. Additionally, they aim to provide interpretable explanations for model decisions rather than just achieving high accuracy.

Method: The authors use an equal-weight logit-mean ensemble of 11 models with orthogonal error modes under 10-fold LPO cross-validation. For explanation, they employ part-masking and counterfactual edits to show that decisions depend on motion-grounded body-region evidence. They compare region saliency with Laban Movement Analysis (LMA) attributes versus classical kinematics using Spearman correlation.

Result: The ensemble achieves 36.80% ± 4.00% per-fold Macro-F1, which is a +11.07 percentage point improvement (+43% relative) over the baseline STGCN++ (25.73% ± 4.03%). Region saliency correlates strongly with LMA attributes (rho = +0.500) but weakly with classical kinematics (rho = +0.033), roughly 15x difference. This alignment holds for the submitted 11-way ensemble (rho = +0.517). The temporal saliency analysis shows a negative result: within-window temporal saliency is diffuse rather than localized.

Conclusion: The conclusion is that ensemble methods with orthogonal error modes can significantly improve performance in challenging emotion classification tasks. More importantly, the explanation suite demonstrates that model decisions are grounded in motion-related body regions that align with LMA attributes, providing interpretable and meaningful explanations for the classification decisions. The temporal analysis reveals that emotion recognition relies on diffuse temporal patterns rather than specific localized moments.

Abstract: We study body-only, 12-class acted-emotion classification from skeleton motion under leave-performer-out (LPO) evaluation, a hard, underdetermined setting: chance is 8.3%, and a protocol-matched reproduced STGCN++ baseline reaches only 25.73 +/- 4.03% Macro-F1. We show that reliable gains come not from a new architecture but from combining eleven models with orthogonal error modes: under 10-fold LPO cross-validation on the labeled training performers, an equal-weight logit-mean ensemble reaches 36.80 +/- 4.00% per-fold Macro-F1, a protocol-matched +11.07 pp (+43% relative) over the same-split reproduced baseline. Our central contribution is a tested explanation suite: for a strong ensemble member, part-masking and counterfactual edits show (rather than assert) that its decisions depend on motion-grounded body-region evidence, and this region saliency aligns with rule-based Laban Movement Analysis (LMA) attributes far more than with classical kinematics: region-level saliency-LMA Spearman rho = +0.500 versus +0.033, roughly 15x, and the alignment holds for the submitted 11-way ensemble itself at rho = +0.517; the audit is post hoc and needs no retraining. The same suite faithfully reports a negative: within-window temporal saliency is diffuse rather than localized.

</details>


### [85] [Beauty is in the AI of the beholder: MLLMs systematically overrate facial attractiveness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02512)
*Santiago Grandas,Juan Sebastian Cely-Acosta,Mohit Mendiratta,Shafee Hassan,Macken Murphy*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This study compares AI beauty assessments from four commercial MLLMs (Claude, Gemini, GPT, Grok) with human ratings of 2,513 participants. While MLLMs show strong correlation with human rank-orderings of facial attractiveness, they systematically rate faces more favorably and within a narrower range than humans.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether Multimodal Large Language Models can accurately reflect human judgments of facial attractiveness, as AI beauty assessments are becoming increasingly popular among users, companies, and aestheticians.

Method: Pre-registered exploratory study comparing attractiveness ratings from 2,513 human participants to four commercial AI models (Claude, Gemini, GPT, Grok).

Result: MLLMs rate faces more favorably and within a narrower range than humans. They accurately track the rank-ordering of faces but not in absolute terms. Only face age predicted attractiveness in both humans and MLLMs. AI models strongly agree with each other, except Grok which showed lowest agreement with humans.

Conclusion: Current off-the-shelf commercial MLLMs can approximate rank-orderings of human attractiveness but systematically overrate facial beauty compared to humans.

Abstract: Beauty assessments from Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) are increasingly popular amongst users, companies, and aestheticians. This raises the question of whether these AI models can accurately reflect human judgments of attractiveness. In a pre- registered exploratory study, we compared the attractiveness ratings of 2,513 human participants to four widely used commercial AI models: Claude, Gemini, GPT, and Grok. Results showed that MLLMs systematically rate faces more favourably and within a narrower range than humans and, at the time of study, do not reproduce human ratings in absolute terms. However, MLLMs exhibit strong correlations with human attractiveness judgments, accurately tracking the rank-ordering of faces. MLLMs may judge faces by different cues than humans; only face age was a predictor of facial attractiveness in both humans and MLLMs, with inconsistent patterns across models for ethnicity and gender. AI models strongly agree with one another, except for Grok, which also showed the lowest agreement with humans. Our findings suggest that while they may be able to approximate rank-orderings of human attractiveness, current off-the-shelf commercial MLLMs systematically overrate the beauty of human faces.

</details>


### [86] [Doppio: A Dataset for Contactless Weight Estimation of Falling Particles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02528)
*Simon Kiefhaber,Jan-Martin O. Steitz,Julia Grabinski,Christoph Reich,Paul Wagner,Max Zimmermann,Simone Schaub-Meyer,Stefan Roth*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes using computer vision with deep learning as a contactless solution for measuring the mass of falling powder particles, using coffee grinding as a case study. The authors introduce a novel dataset called "Doppio" with videos of falling coffee paired with per-frame weight measurements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional scales are effective for static measurements but inadequate for dynamic, contactless scenarios. Existing contactless solutions are costly, application-specific, and technically complex, creating a need for more accessible alternatives.

Method: The authors evaluate multiple deep learning approaches: purely spatial feed-forward networks and recurrent spatio-temporal models. They analyze these models for predictive accuracy and computational trade-offs using their custom "Doppio" dataset.

Result: Deep learning-based computer vision models can accurately estimate the cumulative weight of falling particles, demonstrating the feasibility of vision-based contactless measurement.

Conclusion: Computer vision with deep learning provides a practical foundation for future vision-based contactless measurement solutions in industrial applications.

Abstract: Measuring the mass of powder, including falling particles, is a common task in industrial applications. While scales are effective for static measurements, many applications require contactless sensing, where existing solutions are often costly, application-specific, and technically complex. In this work, we investigate computer vision as a practical alternative for contactless mass estimation. As an accessible real-world case study, we focus on coffee grinding and introduce \emph{Doppio}, a novel video dataset capturing videos of falling ground coffee, paired with precise, per-frame ground-truth weight measurements. To demonstrate contactless measuring, we evaluate deep learning-based approaches ranging from purely spatial feed-forward networks to recurrent spatio-temporal models. These models are analyzed with respect to their predictive accuracy and computational trade-offs. We demonstrate that deep learning-based computer vision models accurately estimate the cumulative weight of falling particles, establishing a solid foundation for future vision-based contactless measurement solutions.

</details>


### [87] [Fine-Grained Anomaly Perception in Wild UGC-Enhanced Images: A Comprehensive Dataset and Difference-Fusion Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02529)
*Yan Zhong,Gefei Chen,Qiufang Ma,Zhen Wang,Zhiwei Fan,Lei Shi,Tingting Jiang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper introduces a new task called Quality Anomaly Perception for UGC image Enhancement (UEAP) to address visual anomalies introduced by enhancement algorithms in user-generated content. The authors contribute UEAP-4k, the first benchmark dataset with fine-grained annotations for anomaly categories, localization, and severity levels. They propose DFAP-UGC method with difference fusion, spatial querying, regional verification, and quality-aware ranking, along with a Locality-Aware Dynamic Task Prioritization (LADTP) training strategy for effective end-to-end learning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing Image Quality Assessment (IQA) methods perform well on classic distortions but fail to capture specific, localized anomalies (in faces, texts, textures) caused by enhancement algorithms in real-world UGC. These anomalies directly undermine perceptual fidelity and viewer trust on short-video and social media platforms.

Method: The proposed DFAP-UGC method leverages: (1) Explicit problem-reference difference fusion with dense spatial querying, (2) Regional verification, and (3) Quality-aware ranking for robust anomaly identification. The LADTP training strategy handles the coupling of subtasks, enabling effective end-to-end learning and eliminating multi-stage overhead.

Result: Extensive experiments show that the proposed method outperforms baselines adapted from classical approaches for this task, validating the value of the UEAP-4k dataset and the superiority of DFAP-UGC for robust UGC-enhanced image anomaly perception.

Conclusion: The paper successfully establishes a new benchmark task (UEAP) and dataset (UEAP-4k) for anomaly perception in enhanced UGC images. The proposed DFAP-UGC method with LADTP training strategy demonstrates superior performance over existing approaches, providing a valuable tool for improving perceptual quality assessment in real-world UGC scenarios.

Abstract: Image enhancement and restoration have become standard back-end operations on short-video and social media platforms to boost UGC visual experience. Yet these processes inevitably introduce visual anomalies--especially in faces, texts, and textures--that directly undermine perceptual fidelity and viewer trust. While existing IQA methods perform well on classic distortions, they target holistic quality assessment and fail to capture the specific, localized anomalies caused by enhancement algorithms in real-world UGC. To bridge this gap, we formally define a new task-quality Anomaly Perception for UGC image Enhancement (UEAP), and contribute the first UEAP benchmark dataset, named UEAP-4k, curated from the real business scenarios. It provides fine-grained annotations for anomaly categories, localization and severity levels. Furthermore, we propose a Difference-Fusion Anomaly Perception Method (DFAP-UGC) for wild UGC-enhanced images, which leverages explicit problem-reference difference fusion with dense spatial querying, regional verification, and quality-aware ranking, enabling robust anomaly identification in challenging scenarios. To handle the inherent coupling of subtasks in this new task, we propose a Locality-Aware Dynamic Task Prioritization (LADTP) training strategy that enables effective end-to-end learning and eliminates multi-stage overhead. Extensive experiments show that our method outperforms baselines adapted from classical approaches for this task, validating the value of this dataset and the superior of DFAP-UGC for robust UGC-enhanced image anomaly perception. Code and data will be public.

</details>


### [88] [Spatially Aware World Action Model via Geometric Latent Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02531)
*Javier Alejandro Lopetegui Gonzalez,Paul Pacaud,Cordelia Schmid*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SA-WAM extends World Action Models (WAMs) by adding depth prediction capabilities, enabling 3D-aware world modeling and action prediction within a single diffusion backbone. It uses a nonlinear encoding to incorporate depth information without sacrificing pretrained priors from video diffusion models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing WAMs operate exclusively on RGB observations and do not leverage 3D information. This limits their ability to understand spatial relationships crucial for robot manipulation. The gap exists because pretrained video models expect bounded input domains, and depth signals are unbounded, making integration challenging without fine-tuning.

Method: SA-WAM repurposes a pretrained video diffusion model for joint action, RGB, and depth prediction. It uses a nonlinear encoding that maps unbounded depth signals into the bounded input domain expected by the frozen VAE tokenizer, allowing reuse of the tokenizer without 3D-specific fine-tuning.

Result: SA-WAM achieves state-of-the-art results on RoboCasa and LIBERO-Plus benchmarks while improving future-state predictions. It outperforms strong baselines in real-world evaluation using a UR5 robotic arm, with strong gains in randomized environments. Analysis shows correlation between world model prediction quality and rollout success.

Conclusion: Incorporating geometric (depth) information through nonlinear encoding preserves pretrained priors while enabling 3D-aware world modeling. This approach provides a viable pathway for enhancing WAMs with spatial awareness, and the correlation between prediction quality and rollout success suggests future improvements should focus on world model accuracy.

Abstract: World Action Models (WAMs) leverage the capabilities of large-scale pretrained video diffusion models to jointly predict future observations and actions, inheriting rich visual and physical priors from internet-scale video. This has made them a promising paradigm for robot policy learning, yet the prevailing models operate exclusively on RGB observations and do not leverage 3D information. To bridge this gap, we introduce a Spatially Aware World Action Model (SA-WAM), which repurposes a pretrained video model for joint action, RGB, and depth prediction, enabling 3D-aware world modeling and action prediction within a single diffusion backbone. We use a nonlinear encoding that maps the unbounded depth signal into the bounded input domain expected by the frozen VAE tokenizer. This allows us to reuse the tokenizer without 3D-specific fine-tuning, incorporating geometric information without sacrificing the pretrained priors. SA-WAM achieves state-of-the-art results on the RoboCasa and LIBERO-Plus benchmarks, while simultaneously improving future-state predictions. Furthermore, SA-WAM outperforms strong baselines in real-world evaluation using a UR5 robotic arm, with strong gains in randomized environments. We analyze the correlation between world model prediction quality and rollout success, providing insights into WAM performance and avenues for its improvement.

</details>


### [89] [RGB-to-IR image translation for infrared vehicle detection in unseen UAV domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02556)
*Thijs A. Eker,Ella P. Fokkinga,Jan Erik van Woerden,Elfi I. S. Hofmeijer,Sebastiaan P. Snel,Klamer Schutte,Friso G. Heslinga*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This work investigates whether modern generative translators can bridge the RGB-to-infrared cross-modal gap to improve infrared vehicle detection on unseen UAV target domains. Stable Diffusion 3.5 with ControlNet achieves the best results, improving mAP from 50.8 to 60.1 on Kust4K and from 25.6 to 38.4 on VTUAV compared to source-domain IR-only training.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Real-world thermal IR data is scarce for aerial vehicle detection, while abundant RGB UAV imagery exists. However, learning RGB-to-IR translation is challenging because thermal traits (e.g., engine heat) are unobservable in RGB images. This work investigates whether modern generative models can overcome this cross-modal gap.

Method: Generative translators (supervised GANs, ControlNet-based diffusion models, and LoRA-based foundation model editing) are trained on paired RGB-IR source datasets and applied to RGB training images from held-out target datasets to generate synthetic IR data. RF-DETR vehicle detectors trained on synthetic IR are evaluated on unseen IR target test splits across five aerial datasets, with Kust4K and VTUAV as target domains.

Result: Stable Diffusion 3.5 with ControlNet yields the best results. mAP improved from 50.8 to 60.1 on Kust4K and from 25.6 to 38.4 on VTUAV compared to source-domain IR-only training. Multiple seeds (+1.1 mAP) and prompt variations (+3.3 mAP) provide additional gains on VTUAV. Synthetic IR consistently outperforms RGB and grayscale baselines, though a performance gap to real target IR data remains.

Conclusion: Generative RGB-to-IR translation effectively mitigates IR data scarcity and improves cross-domain aerial vehicle detection, despite a persistent gap to real target IR data.

Abstract: Synthetic training data is crucial for developing vision AI when real-world data is scarce, as in thermal infrared (IR) aerial vehicle detection. While abundant UAV RGB imagery motivates RGB-to-IR translation for data augmentation, unobservable thermal traits (e.g., engine heat) make learning transferable mappings challenging. This work investigates whether modern generative translators can overcome this cross-modal gap to improve infrared vehicle detection on unseen UAV target domains. Translators are trained on paired RGB-IR source datasets and applied to RGB training images from held-out target datasets to generate synthetic IR data. Evaluated methods include supervised GANs, ControlNet-based diffusion models, and foundation-model editing via LoRA. The resulting synthetic IR imagery is used to train RF-DETR vehicle detectors, which are evaluated on unseen IR target test splits across five aerial datasets, with Kust4K and VTUAV serving as target domains. Synthetic IR consistently outperforms RGB and grayscale baselines. Stable Diffusion 3.5 with ControlNet yields the best results, improving mAP from 50.8 to 60.1 on Kust4K and from 25.6 to 38.4 on VTUAV compared to models trained only on source-domain IR data. Increasing output diversity via multiple seeds (+1.1 mAP) and prompt variations (+3.3 mAP) provides additional gains on VTUAV. Although a performance gap to real target IR data remains, generative RGB-to-IR translation effectively mitigates IR data scarcity and improves cross-domain aerial vehicle detection.

</details>


### [90] [Stereo 4D Radar for 3D Object Detection: Integrating Geometric Alignment and Absolute Velocity Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02560)
*Seung-Hyun Song,Dong-Hee Paek,Woong-Chan Byun,Seung-Hyun Kong*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a stereo 4D Radar-based 3D object detection framework that uses geometric disparity between left and right Radars to estimate absolute velocity and fuse complementary features, achieving significant improvements over state-of-the-art mono 4D Radar baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 4D Radar faces challenges including clutter from road surfaces/guardrails/vehicles, multipath ghost reflections, inherent noise, resulting in sparse data after preprocessing. Additionally, Doppler measurements only capture radial velocity, limiting full motion state recovery. Stereo radar geometry can address these limitations.

Method: The framework exploits geometric disparity between left and right 4D Radars to estimate absolute velocity of objects and achieves more robust perception through fusion of complementary features from both radar sensors.

Result: Validated on in-house stereo 4D Radar dataset, achieving performance gains of 8.82 points in AP_3D and 9.0 points in AP_BEV over state-of-the-art mono 4D Radar baselines.

Conclusion: Absolute velocity estimation combined with stereo geometry-aware feature fusion leads to substantial improvements in 3D object detection, demonstrating the effectiveness of stereo 4D Radar for robust perception.

Abstract: Four-dimensional (4D) Radar is a powerful sensing modality capable of detecting surrounding three-dimensional (3D) objects under diverse weather conditions and providing Doppler-based motion information. However, raw 4D Radar signals contain significant clutter from road surfaces, guardrails, and surrounding vehicles, along with multipath-induced ghost reflections and the receiver's inherent noise floor. Consequently, preprocessing algorithms designed to remove such invalid measurements often make the Radar data excessively sparse. Moreover, the Doppler measurements provided by 4D Radar describe only the radial component of an object's velocity, limiting their ability to recover the full motion state. In this paper, we introduce a stereo 4D Radar-based 3D object detection framework that exploits the geometric disparity between left and right Radars to estimate the absolute velocity of objects and achieve more robust perception through the fusion of their complementary features. The effectiveness of the proposed framework is validated on our in-house stereo 4D Radar dataset, demonstrating performance gains of 8.82 points in AP 3D and 9.0 points in AP BEV over state-of-the-art mono 4D Radar baselines. These results demonstrate that absolute velocity estimation combined with stereo geometry-aware feature fusion leads to substantial improvements in 3D object detection.

</details>


### [91] [MARS: What Retrieval Signals Are Hidden in Multimodal Large Language Models for Text-Video Retrieval?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02565)
*Uicheol Jung,Juyoung Hong,Geuntaek Lim,Yukyung Choi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MARS proposes a multi-layer and multi-slot embedding framework for text-video retrieval that addresses the limitation of single-token representations in multimodal LLMs by combining hidden states from different decoder layers to create multiple adaptive representation slots, with a hard-negative-aware slot specialization objective for discriminative matching.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current multimodal large language models adapted as embedding models for text-video retrieval represent each input using a single token from the final layer, which compresses diverse video-text cues into a single vector and limits fine-grained retrieval, especially for confusing candidates with similar scenes, actions, and temporal patterns.

Method: MARS constructs multiple adaptive representation slots by combining hidden states from different decoder layers, compares corresponding text and video slots, and aggregates their similarities for retrieval. Additionally, a hard-negative-aware slot specialization objective is introduced to encourage slots to capture discriminative matching cues for better handling of confusing candidates.

Result: MARS achieves state-of-the-art results in both direct similarity-based retrieval and reranking settings on four text-video retrieval benchmarks. Ablation studies show that multi-layer fusion, multiple slots, and hard-negative-aware slot specialization provide complementary gains.

Conclusion: The proposed MARS framework effectively addresses the single-token representation limitation in multimodal LLM-based embeddings for text-video retrieval through multi-layer fusion and multiple slots with hard-negative-aware training, demonstrating superior performance across multiple benchmarks.

Abstract: Text-video retrieval requires representations that can distinguish videos with similar scenes, actions, and temporal patterns. Recent multimodal large language models have been adapted as embedding models, but they often represent each input using a single token from the final layer. This can compress diverse video-text cues into a single vector and limit fine-grained retrieval. To address this limitation, we propose MARS, a multi-layer and multi-slot embedding framework for text-video retrieval. MARS constructs multiple adaptive representation slots by combining hidden states from different decoder layers, compares corresponding text and video slots, and aggregates their similarities for retrieval. To better handle confusing candidates, we further introduce a hard-negative-aware slot specialization objective that encourages the slots to capture discriminative matching cues. Experiments on four text-video retrieval benchmarks show that MARS achieves state-of-the-art results in both direct similarity-based retrieval and reranking settings. Ablation studies and analyses demonstrate that multi-layer fusion, multiple slots, and hard-negative-aware slot specialization provide complementary gains. Code is available at https://github.com/sejong-rcv/MARS.

</details>


### [92] [Deeply Interleaved Text-Image Contexts for Multimodal LLMs Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02573)
*Zihao Wang,Xi Xiang,Yuwen Sun,Yingyu Li,Yabo Zhang,Yihan Zeng,Fan Li,Wangmeng Zuo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TIC-Bench is a novel benchmark designed to evaluate multimodal large language models (MLLMs) on their ability to understand deeply interleaved text-image contexts, addressing the gap in current evaluations that focus predominantly on multi-image tasks with minimal text-image interaction. The benchmark covers Logical, Temporal, and Spatial Association domains with 2,280 questions across eight specific task types.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current multimodal model evaluations and training primarily focus on multi-image tasks where text serves only as instructions, lacking deep semantic interaction with visual content. However, real-world applications like text-image co-creation, character tracking, and spatial reconstruction require constant text-image interaction. This gap necessitates a benchmark to evaluate models' capabilities in deeply interleaved text-image contexts.

Method: The researchers developed TIC-Bench (deeply interleaved Text-Image Contexts) benchmark comprising three core domains (Logical, Temporal, and Spatial Association) with eight specific task types and 2,280 questions. They evaluated 10 state-of-the-art MLLMs against human experts to assess their ability to integrate text-image clues and recover ground truth facts within deeply interleaved contexts.

Result: The evaluation revealed a substantial performance gap between the 10 state-of-the-art MLLMs and human experts. Models demonstrated persistent difficulties in integrating evidence distributed across interleaved visual and textual inputs, indicating significant room for improvement in handling deeply interleaved text-image contexts.

Conclusion: TIC-Bench provides a valuable analytical tool for assessing and advancing multimodal models' ability to effectively integrate text and image information in deeply interleaved contexts. The benchmark reveals substantial deficiencies in current MLLMs, highlighting the need for future research to develop models with stronger text-image integration capabilities for real-world applications.

Abstract: Current evaluations and training of multimodal models predominantly focus on multi-image tasks, largely overlooking interleaved text-image scenarios.
