In such multi-image tasks, text typically serves merely as task instructions, lacking deep semantic interaction with the visual content. In contrast, realworld applications like text-image co-creation, character tracking, and spatial reconstruction require constant interaction between text and images. Consequently, models must possess a deep understanding of these interleaved contexts. To bridge this gap, we introduce a novel benchmark, TIC-Bench (deeply interleaved Text-Image Contexts), designed to evaluate the capability of models to integrate text-image clues and recover the ground truth facts within deeply interleaved contexts. This benchmark encompasses three core domains: Logical, Temporal, and Spatial Association, which are further categorized into eight specific types, comprising a total of 2,280 questions. We evaluated 10 state-of-the-art MLLMs and observed a substantial performance gap compared to human experts, together with persistent difficulties in integrating evidence distributed across interleaved visual and textual inputs. Ultimately, this benchmark provides a valuable analytical tool for assessing and advancing the ability of multimodal models to effectively integrate text and image information in deeply interleaved contexts. TIC-Bench is publicly available at https://huggingface.co/datasets/pino10010/TIC-Bench

</details>


### [93] [Generalizable Brain Tumor Segmentation with Self-Training and Tumor-Aware Deformations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02600)
*Henrique Zan Grande,Jeovane Honorio Alves,Rayson Laroca,Andre Gustavo Hochuli*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper presents a solution for the BraTS-GoAT 2026 challenge, focusing on robust brain tumor sub-region segmentation across heterogeneous patient populations. The method combines nnU-Net with a large residual encoder, semi-supervised learning using pseudo-labels, and a novel tumor-aware deformable augmentation. The approach achieves strong generalization performance with Dice scores of 0.881 (Whole Tumor), 0.817 (Tumor Core), and 0.775 (Enhancing Tumor), outperforming labeled-only baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The BraTS-GoAT task addresses the challenge of generalizing brain tumor segmentation across heterogeneous patient populations. This is clinically important because models trained on specific patient cohorts often fail to generalize to new populations with different demographic or disease characteristics. The motivation is to develop a robust segmentation method that can perform well across diverse patient groups, which is essential for clinical deployment.

Method: The proposed method integrates three key components: (1) nnU-Net framework with a large residual encoder architecture for robust segmentation; (2) semi-supervised learning technique using pseudo-labels generated from unlabeled training data, with varying proportions of the most confident pseudo-labeled cases; (3) tumor-aware deformable augmentation that locally deforms the lesion region while preserving the surrounding anatomy, ensuring realistic transformations.

Result: The submitted configuration achieved the following scores on the BraTS-GoAT validation set: Whole Tumor (Dice: 0.881, NSD: 0.473), Tumor Core (Dice: 0.817, NSD: 0.490), and Enhancing Tumor (Dice: 0.775, NSD: 0.533). The method improved over labeled-only baselines across all tumor regions, demonstrating the complementary nature of self-training and the proposed tumor-aware augmentation.

Conclusion: The study demonstrates that combining nnU-Net with semi-supervised learning (pseudo-labels) and tumor-aware deformable augmentation effectively improves brain tumor segmentation generalization across heterogeneous patient populations. The individual components are complementary, with self-training leveraging unlabeled data and the augmentation strategy preserving anatomical consistency while increasing data diversity. The approach achieves state-of-the-art performance on the BraTS-GoAT 2026 generalization task.

Abstract: This work presents an approach to the Generalizability Across Tumors (BraTS-GoAT) task of the BraTS 2026 Challenge, which focuses on robust segmentation of brain tumor sub-regions across a heterogeneous patient population. The proposed method employs the nnU-Net framework with a large residual encoder architecture, integrating a semi-supervised learning technique with pseudo-labels generated from the unlabeled training data and a tumor-aware deformable augmentation that locally deforms the lesion while preserving the surrounding anatomy. We evaluate the individual contributions of each component, as well as their combination, using varying proportions of the most confident pseudo-labeled cases. The submitted configuration for the generalization task achieves Dice and NSD scores of 0.881 and 0.473 for Whole Tumor, 0.817 and 0.490 for Tumor Core, and 0.775 and 0.533 for Enhancing Tumor on the BraTS-GoAT validation set, improving over the labeled-only baselines across all tumor regions and confirming that self-training and the proposed augmentation are complementary. Our source code is publicly available at https://github.com/Henrique-zan/brats-goat-2026/.

</details>


### [94] [AffectDelta: Beyond Emotion Labels for Image Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02616)
*Xingzu Zhan,Lin Gu,Ruogu Fang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AffectDelta is a source-aware image editing framework that treats emotion-driven editing as a transition between eight-dimensional emotion distributions, rather than using single emotion categories. It uses a frozen Emotion Distribution Predictor to estimate source emotion state, encodes the source-to-target difference as direction and magnitude, and employs a transition encoder and source-aware diffusion backbone to generate context-dependent semantic and appearance changes. Trained on AffectPair-249K dataset with 248,841 source-target pairs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing scene-level emotion editors use single emotion categories which collapse mixed affective endpoints into one dominant label, and language-based instructions cannot precisely quantify how coexisting emotions should change. The authors aim to address these limitations by treating editing as a transition between multi-dimensional emotion distributions.

Method: 1) Use a frozen Emotion Distribution Predictor to estimate 8-dimensional emotion distribution of source image
2) Encode signed source-to-target difference to capture direction and magnitude of requested transition
3) Build internal transition encoder to process this signal
4) Combine with source-aware diffusion backbone to generate context-dependent semantic and appearance changes
5) Train on AffectPair-249K dataset with 248,841 source-target pairs spanning cross-category and within-category transitions

Result: Experiments against six baselines show improved affective alignment and content preservation. Ablations validate the design choices. The method outperforms existing approaches by treating emotion editing as distribution transitions rather than category-based editing.

Conclusion: AffectDelta successfully addresses the limitations of category-based and language-based emotion editing by representing emotions as 8-dimensional distributions and modeling editing as a transition process. The source-aware approach with transition encoder and diffusion backbone enables context-dependent semantic and appearance changes while preserving overall composition and semantic-structural coherence.

Abstract: Emotion-driven image editing aims to evoke a specified target emotion by modifying emotion-relevant visual cues in a source image, while preserving the overall composition and semantic-structural coherence of the original scene. Existing scene-level editors typically specify the target with a single emotion category and often learn visual transformations from operation-level text instructions. A category collapses a mixed affective endpoint into one dominant label, while language cannot precisely quantify how coexisting emotions should increase, decrease, or remain stable. We introduce AffectDelta, a source-aware editor that treats editing as a transition between eight-dimensional emotion distributions. A frozen Emotion Distribution Predictor estimates the source state, and the signed source-to-target difference encodes the direction and magnitude of the requested transition. Within AffectDelta, an internal transition encoder and a source-aware diffusion backbone jointly translate this signal into context-dependent semantic and appearance changes. To train this formulation, we construct AffectPair-249K, comprising 248,841 source-target pairs with predicted eight-dimensional distributions and spanning both cross-category and within-category transitions. Experiments against six baselines, combining quantitative evaluation with qualitative comparisons, demonstrate improved affective alignment and content preservation, while ablations validate our design choices. Code and dataset will be made publicly available upon acceptance.

</details>


### [95] [From Detection to Localization: A Unified Forensics Framework for Fully Synthetic and Tampered Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02640)
*Annalisa Gallina,Marco Fiorucci,Marco Brigo,Federica Battisti,Lamberto Ballan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a unified multiclass framework for detecting and localizing manipulated images, addressing the limitations of binary classification approaches by distinguishing between real, fully generated, and tampered images, while also enabling pixel-level localization of tampered regions through a segmentation branch.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The rapid advancement of generative models has created highly realistic forgeries, making image manipulation detection increasingly challenging. Conventional binary classification approaches (real vs. generated) cannot distinguish between different types of manipulation or localize tampered regions.

Method: The authors extend an existing detector by introducing a unified multiclass framework with three categories: real vs. fully generated vs. tampered. The framework incorporates a segmentation branch for pixel-level localization of tampered regions alongside classification.

Result: The proposed approach outperforms selected recent benchmarks, achieving improved classification accuracy and higher IoU scores for the localization task.

Conclusion: The unified framework offers an efficient solution that combines classification and localization capabilities for detecting both fully synthetic and tampered images, outperforming existing methods on multiple evaluation metrics.

Abstract: The rapid advancement of generative models has significantly worsened the problem of manipulated image detection, as these methods are capable of producing highly realistic forgeries, reinforcing the importance of multimedia forensics. Conventional approaches typically frame image manipulation detection as a binary classification task (real vs. generated), which limits the capability to distinguish and localize different forms of manipulation. To address these constraints, this work extends an existing detector by introducing a unified multiclass framework (real vs. fully generated vs. tampered). In addition to classifying image authenticity, the framework incorporates a segmentation branch to enable pixel-level localization of tampered regions. The proposed approach outperforms selected recent benchmarks, offering an efficient solution with improved classification accuracy and higher IoU scores for the localization task. Find the code at https://github.com/anngal01/From-Detection-to-Localization-A-Unified-Forensics-Framework-for-Fully-Synthetic-and-Tampered-Images.

</details>


### [96] [Learning to Attract and Repel: Dual Quality Margin Learning for Face Recognition (DQM-Face)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02644)
*El Ouanas Belabbaci,Bhavesh Wani,Philipp Terhörst*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DQM-Face proposes dual quality margin learning for face recognition by combining magnitude-based quality estimation with semantic quality learning via squeeze-and-excitation attention. The method uses quality-aware margins for intra-class attraction and repulsion margins for inter-class separation, achieving state-of-the-art performance while also being effective for face image quality assessment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Face recognition in unconstrained environments faces challenges from diverse variations. Existing margin-based approaches rely on feature magnitude for sample quality modeling, but magnitude-based modeling alone is susceptible to identity-agnostic noise, which degrades the reliability and discriminative power of learned representations.

Method: The paper proposes Dual Quality Margin Learning for Face Recognition (DQM-Face), which unifies conventional magnitude-based quality estimation with a newly introduced semantic quality learning mechanism realized via squeeze-and-excitation semantic attention. By jointly leveraging magnitude and semantic cues, the method constructs enhanced quality-aware margins that adaptively strengthen intra-class compactness through improved attraction. Additionally, a repulsion margin formulation explicitly enlarges inter-class separation.

Result: Extensive experiments on multiple challenging benchmarks demonstrate that DQM-Face consistently outperforms state-of-the-art face recognition methods. The learned quality signal is also highly effective for face image quality assessment, demonstrating intrinsic alignment with the recognition objective.

Conclusion: The unified integration of semantic quality modeling with dual attraction-repulsion margin optimization results in a more structured and discriminative feature geometry. The approach achieves superior recognition performance while simultaneously enabling effective face image quality assessment.

Abstract: Face recognition in unconstrained environments remains highly challenging due to diverse and extreme variations encountered in real-world scenarios. To mitigate these effects, existing margin-based approaches model sample quality through feature magnitude. However, magnitude-based modeling alone is susceptible to identity-agnostic noise, which can degrade the reliability and discriminative power of learned representations. In this paper, we propose Dual Quality Margin Learning for Face Recognition (DQM-Face), a novel framework that enables refined attraction and repulsion dynamics during representation learning. Our approach unifies conventional magnitude-based quality estimation with a newly introduced semantic quality learning mechanism, realized via squeeze-and-excitation semantic attention. By jointly leveraging magnitude and semantic cues, we construct enhanced quality-aware margins that adaptively strengthen intra-class compactness through improved attraction during learning. To further enhance inter-class discrimination, we introduce a repulsion margin formulation that explicitly enlarges inter-class separation. The unified integration of semantic quality modeling with dual attraction-repulsion margin optimization results in a more structured and discriminative feature geometry. Extensive experiments on multiple challenging benchmarks demonstrate that DQM-Face consistently outperforms state-of-the-art face recognition methods. Moreover, we show that the quality learned for margin optimization is highly effective for face image quality assessment within the proposed framework, demonstrating that the learned quality signal is intrinsically aligned with the recognition objective. The code is publicly available: https://github.com/RAIB-group/DQM-Face

</details>


### [97] [Physics-Driven Independent Pair Generation for Iterative Self-Supervised Low-Dose CT Denoising](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02654)
*Xianlei Han,Shaoyu Wang,Jiancheng Fang,Weiwen Wu,Qiegen Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A physics-driven self-supervised learning framework for low-dose CT (LDCT) denoising that explicitly models mixed Poisson-Gaussian noise through cross-domain iteration, achieving performance comparable to supervised methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: LDCT measurements contain mixed Poisson-Gaussian noise, but existing self-supervised methods rely on generic image statistics without explicitly modeling this noise, limiting their effectiveness in suppressing realistic LDCT noise.

Method: Three-step framework: (1) Use learned sinogram prior and LDCT noise model for posterior inference to separate Poisson and Gaussian components; (2) Process components via binomial thinning and Gaussian data thinning with residual scaling to create training pairs with independent noise realizations; (3) Train image-domain network whose forward-projected outputs update the prior through cross-domain iteration.

Result: Experiments on simulated data (AAPM, LIDC-IDRI, LoDoPaB-CT) and real LDCT data show consistent gains over self-supervised baselines across dose levels, with performance comparable to supervised baseline.

Conclusion: The physics-driven approach with explicit noise modeling and cross-domain iteration effectively addresses LDCT denoising, achieving superior performance over generic self-supervised methods and matching supervised approaches.

Abstract: Low-dose computed tomography (LDCT) measurements contain mixed Poisson-Gaussian noise. However, most self-supervised methods rely on generic image statistics and do not explicitly model this noise, which may limit their ability to effectively suppress realistic LDCT noise. To address this issue, we propose a physics-driven framework with cross-domain iteration for self-supervised LDCT denoising. The proposed framework proceeds in three main steps. First, a learned sinogram prior and the LDCT noise model guide posterior inference of photon counts, enabling separation of the Poisson and Gaussian components. Second, the separated Poisson and Gaussian components are respectively processed by binomial thinning and Gaussian data thinning to construct two branches, and residual scaling matches each branch's noise level to that of the observation, yielding a training pair with approximately independent noise realizations from one low-dose measurement. Finally, the pair is used to train an image-domain network whose forward-projected outputs update the prior. Through cross-domain iteration, the prior and the training pair are progressively refined while maintaining consistency with CT acquisition physics. Experiments on simulated data from AAPM, LIDC-IDRI, and LoDoPaB-CT and on real LDCT data show consistent gains over the evaluated self-supervised baselines across dose levels, with performance comparable to the evaluated supervised baseline.

</details>


### [98] [Characterizing Text Branch Sensitivity in Medical Vision-Language Segmentation via Evidence Decoupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02663)
*Ziquan Liu,Zhewei Zhu,Xuyang Shi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper investigates how textual information contributes to pixel-level predictions in multimodal medical image segmentation using pretrained VLMs. The authors propose an Evidence Decoupling Decoder (EDD) to analyze modality interactions and find that text sensitivity varies significantly across datasets - some show catastrophic performance drops without text while others show marginal impact.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is to address the gap in understanding how much textual information actually contributes to pixel-level predictions in medical image segmentation with VLMs, despite their promising performance when incorporating clinical text.

Method: The authors: 1) Analyzed several commonly used fusion strategies to test segmentation performance sensitivity; 2) Proposed an Evidence Decoupling Decoder (EDD) based on evidential deep learning and deep supervision, which serves as an internal representation analysis tool to decompose image evidence and text-modulated evidence throughout the decoding process while maintaining competitive segmentation performance.

Result: Key findings: 1) Segmentation performance is largely insensitive to the choice of fusion module; 2) Text sensitivity varies substantially across datasets - BUSI and BTMRI show catastrophic performance drops when text is removed (strong model reliance), while ISIC and Kvasir-SEG show relatively marginal text influence; 3) Text affects predictions mainly through global semantic modulation rather than independent spatial localization; 4) The specific semantic components driving text sensitivity differ across datasets.

Conclusion: The findings provide deeper understanding of modality interaction in multimodal medical image segmentation and offer practical insights for future model design, particularly highlighting that text's role varies by dataset and operates through global semantic modulation rather than spatial localization.

Abstract: Pretrained vision-language models (VLMs) have shown promising performance in medical image segmentation by incorporating clinical text. However, it remains unclear how much textual information actually contributes to pixel-level predictions. In this work, we systematically investigate the role of text in multimodal medical image segmentation. We first analyze several commonly used fusion strategies and find that segmentation performance is largely insensitive to the choice of fusion module. To further understand modality interactions, we propose an Evidence Decoupling Decoder (EDD) based on evidential deep learning and deep supervision. EDD serves as an internal representation analysis tool that decomposes image evidence and text-modulated evidence throughout the decoding process while maintaining competitive segmentation performance. Experimental results show that the sensitivity to text perturbation varies substantially across datasets. On BUSI and BTMRI, removing text causes catastrophic performance drops, indicating strong model reliance on textual input. On ISIC and Kvasir-SEG, text exerts relatively marginal influence. We further find that text affects predictions mainly through global semantic modulation rather than independent spatial localization, and that the specific semantic components driving text sensitivity differ across datasets. These findings provide a deeper understanding of modality interaction in multimodal medical image segmentation and offer practical insights for future model design.

</details>


### [99] [Query Rewriting for Complex Object Segmentation in 4D Gaussian Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02664)
*Thanh-Khoi Nguyen,Thien-Phuc Tran,Minh-Triet Tran*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper addresses the problem of verbose language queries degrading performance in 4D Gaussian-based dynamic scene understanding. The authors propose a training-free query rewriting method that converts long descriptive queries into concise keyword-grounded forms, achieving significant improvements in temporal localization (60.92%→92.21%) and spatial segmentation (vIoU: 20.08%→76.94%) on HyperNeRF and Neu3D datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current 4D Gaussian representation frameworks for language-guided dynamic scene understanding are highly sensitive to verbose, narrative-style queries containing noisy contextual information. This limits their practical applicability for complex object segmentation tasks.

Method: A training-free reinterpretation strategy that progressively reduces linguistic noise while preserving semantic anchors relevant to object-centric representations. The approach transforms long descriptive queries into concise keyword-grounded forms.

Result: The method improves average temporal accuracy from 60.92% to 92.21% and average vIoU from 20.08% to 76.94% without any additional fine-tuning. Shorter, keyword-focused queries yield stable video-feature similarity distributions and better alignment with object-centric Gaussian representations.

Conclusion: Query rewriting is an effective approach for improving language-guided 4D Gaussian representations. Concise keyword-grounded queries significantly enhance both temporal localization and spatial segmentation performance, offering a simple yet powerful solution that does not require model retraining.

Abstract: Recent 4D Gaussian representation frameworks have demonstrated strong performance in language-guided dynamic scene understanding. However, these methods remain highly sensitive to verbose and narrative-style queries that contain noisy contextual information. In this paper, we investigate the impact of query rewriting for complex object segmentation in 4D Gaussian representations. Inspired by recent findings in retrieval-augmented language models and keyword-guided query reformulation, we propose a training-free reinterpretation strategy that transforms long descriptive queries into concise keyword-grounded forms. Our approach progressively reduces linguistic noise while preserving semantic anchors relevant to object-centric representations. Experiments on HyperNeRF and Neu3D demonstrate that concise rewritten queries significantly improve both temporal localization and spatial segmentation performance. In particular, our method improves average temporal accuracy from 60.92% to 92.21% and average vIoU from 20.08% to 76.94% without any additional fine-tuning. Extensive ablation studies further reveal that shorter, keyword-focused queries consistently yield stable video-feature similarity distributions and better alignment with object-centric Gaussian representations

</details>


### [100] [Genesis: A Generative Engine for Hierarchical Satellite Image Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02683)
*Subash Khanal,Yangzhi Cui,Daniel Cher,Eric Xing,Brian Wei,Srikumar Sastry,Nathan Jacobs*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper introduces a new task called "multi-scale tile completion" for satellite imagery generation, where given sparse seed tiles at arbitrary zoom levels, a complete and consistent quadtree pyramid must be synthesized. The proposed solution, Genesis, combines vertical super-resolution and horizontal mask-based outpainting operators over the quadtree structure. The authors also contribute Dense500, a multi-scale pyramid dataset, and evaluation metrics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current generative models for satellite imagery operate along a single axis (either zoom to enhance resolution or pan to extend imagery at fixed scale). No existing method produces a complete pyramid that stays consistent across both scale and space, where high-zoom tiles must agree with coarse context and neighboring tiles.

Method: Genesis composes two specialized operators over the quadtree: (1) a vertical super-resolution model for upsampling across zoom levels, and (2) a horizontal mask-based outpainting model for seamless tile stitching. Each operator achieves state-of-the-art results on its respective subtask.

Result: The engine can propagate sparse seeds into seamless, multi-resolution maps from any initial configuration. Each operator achieves state-of-the-art results on its subtask, producing pyramids that are consistent across zoom levels and seamless across neighboring tiles.

Conclusion: Genesis successfully addresses the multi-scale tile completion task by combining vertical super-resolution and horizontal outpainting. The introduced Dense500 dataset and pyramid-level metrics enable proper evaluation of multi-scale consistency in satellite imagery generation.

Abstract: Earth observation is fundamentally multi-scale; geospatial tasks span varied resolutions, and satellite imagery is organized into cascading tile pyramids that nest fine detail within wide coverage. Current generative models of satellite imagery, however, operate along a single axis: they either zoom to enhance a single tile's resolution or pan to extend imagery at a fixed scale. As a result, no existing method produces a complete pyramid that stays consistent across both scale and space, where a high-zoom tile must agree with the coarse context it refines and with the neighbors it meets. Motivated by this gap, we introduce a new task, multi-scale tile completion: given a sparse set of seed tiles at arbitrary zoom levels and positions, synthesize a complete, uniform quadtree that is globally consistent across both scale and space. We approach this task with Genesis, a generative engine that brings both axes together by composing two specialized operators over the quadtree: a vertical super-resolution model and a horizontal mask-based outpainting model, producing pyramids that are consistent across zoom levels and seamless across neighboring tiles. Each operator achieves state-of-the-art results on its subtask, and the engine propagates sparse seeds into seamless, multi-resolution maps from any initial configuration. To evaluate the task and benchmark Genesis, we introduce dense500, a fully observed multi-scale pyramid dataset spanning diverse geographic regions, together with a suite of pyramid-level metrics. Code, models, and our dataset are available at https://github.com/mvrl/genesis.

</details>


### [101] [GaLe: memory-efficient Global Approximate and Local Exact features](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02689)
*Alberto Ancilotto,Elisabetta Farella*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GaLe is a memory-efficient inference technique for deploying pretrained neural networks on embedded devices without retraining. It partitions feature maps into local exact (Le) and global approximate (Ga) components to balance computational efficiency with accuracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Embedded devices lack GPU resources, and existing inference methods suffer from either high computational overhead (patch-based) or accuracy loss (approximation-based). There is a need for a method that achieves both efficiency and accuracy on constrained devices.

Method: GaLe partitions feature maps into two components: (1) Local exact (Le) representation preserving fine details, and (2) Global approximate (Ga) representation retaining long-range dependencies. Unlike standard tiling, GaLe supports global operations and attention mechanisms in hybrid CNN-transformer models.

Result: Validated on ImageNet, GaLe matches exact-inference performance while achieving up to 65% speedup and 90% RAM reduction on Cortex-M33 compared to patch-based inference. Demonstrated across classification, detection, and generation tasks.

Conclusion: GaLe provides a practical solution for deploying pretrained networks on resource-constrained devices without retraining. Its versatility across multiple tasks highlights potential as a foundation for resource-efficient architecture design.

Abstract: Embedded devices typically lack the resources of GPU-equipped machines, and existing inference methods suffer from either high computational overhead (patch-based) or accuracy loss (approximation-based). We propose GaLe, a memory-efficient technique that enables the deployment of pretrained networks on constrained devices without retraining. GaLe partitions feature maps into two components: a local exact (Le) representation that preserves fine details and a global approximate (Ga) representation that retains long-range dependencies. Unlike standard tiling, GaLe supports global operations and attention mechanisms found in hybrid CNN-transformer models. Validated on ImageNet, our method matches exact-inference performance while achieving up to 65% speedup and 90% RAM reduction on a Cortex-M33 compared to patch-based inference. We further demonstrate GaLe's versatility across classification, detection, and generation tasks, highlighting its potential as a foundation for resource-efficient architecture design.

</details>


### [102] [RVSD: Retrieval Vision Sparse Decoding for Mitigating Visual Hallucinations in Large Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02731)
*Canjie Liu,Jiawen Kang,Jinbo Wen,Zishao Zhong*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RVSD is a training-free, plug-and-play decoding framework that mitigates visual hallucinations in vision-language models by unifying token sparsification and Semantic-Space Visual Retrieval (SSVR) in a single decoding pass.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Large vision-language models suffer from Visual Hallucinations (VHs) that undermine reliability in real-world applications. Existing solutions require curated datasets, additional training, or multi-round decoding, incurring significant computational overhead.

Method: The paper proposes RVSD framework with two key components: 1) Semantics-directed token selection strategy that selectively sparsifies redundant tokens while preserving critical visual information; 2) SSVR mechanism that reformulates visual compensation as an on-demand cross-modal retrieval process within a shared semantic space.

Result: RVSD achieves state-of-the-art performance in mitigating VHs while maintaining robust suppression capabilities under long-context generation settings.

Conclusion: The proposed RVSD framework provides an effective training-free solution for reducing visual hallucinations in vision-language models, with strong performance maintained even in long-context generation scenarios.

Abstract: Large vision-language models have achieved remarkable success in vision-language tasks. However, they remain prone to Visual Hallucinations (VHs), undermining their reliability in real-world applications. Existing solutions typically require curated datasets, additional training, or multi-round decoding, resulting in considerable computational overhead. In this paper, we propose \textbf{RVSD} (\underline{R}etrieval \underline{V}ision \underline{S}parse \underline{D}ecoding), a training-free and plug-and-play decoding framework that, for the first time, unifies token sparsification and \textbf{Semantic-Space Visual Retrieval} (SSVR) within a single decoding pass. Within RVSD, we introduce a \textbf{semantics-directed token selection} strategy that selectively sparsifies redundant tokens while preserving critical visual information. We further propose the SSVR mechanism, which reformulates visual compensation as an on-demand cross-modal retrieval process within a shared semantic space. Extensive experiments demonstrate that RVSD achieves state-of-the-art performance in mitigating VHs while maintaining robust suppression capabilities under long-context generation settings. Our code is available here.\footnote{https://github.com/canjie-liu/RVSD}

</details>


### [103] [Generating Medical Image Counterfactuals using Causal Explanations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02697)
*David A. Kelly,Tom Yaacov,Nathan Blake,Sander Beckers,Hana Chockler*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel counterfactual generation framework for medical image diagnosis that does not require generative models. Instead, it extracts causal evidence directly from the classifier to construct counterfactual images, providing a more transparent and deterministic approach to understanding model decisions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Deep learning models in medical imaging lack explainability, limiting their clinical adoption. Existing counterfactual methods rely on auxiliary generative models (GANs, diffusion models), which introduce additional black-box components and make it difficult to distinguish the classifier's true decision-making from the generator's inductive biases.

Method: The proposed framework extracts causal evidence directly from the classifier itself to construct counterfactual images. This approach is deterministic, requires no additional model training, and enables controllable edits within user-specified regions of interest.

Result: Experiments on real-world medical imaging datasets demonstrate that the method successfully changes classifier predictions while remaining closer to the original image than generative baselines.

Conclusion: The proposed method provides a more direct and transparent view of the classifier's decision boundary by avoiding auxiliary generative models, offering a more interpretable and trustworthy approach to auditing deep learning classifiers in medical imaging applications.

Abstract: Deep learning models have achieved impressive performance in medical image diagnosis, yet their deployment in clinical settings remains constrained by limited explainability. Counterfactual images provide one means of auditing model behavior by showing how an image would need to change for a classifier to produce a different prediction. Existing approaches typically generate such explanations using auxiliary models, including generative adversarial networks and diffusion models. While often capable of producing visually realistic images, these methods explain one black-box model using another, making it difficult to separate the classifier's decision-making process from the inductive biases of the generator.
  We propose a novel counterfactual-generation framework that requires no generative model. Instead, counterfactuals are constructed directly from causal evidence extracted from the classifier. The resulting approach is deterministic, requires no additional model training, and enables controllable edits within user-specified regions of interest. Experiments on real-world medical imaging datasets demonstrate that the proposed method successfully changes classifier predictions while remaining closer to the original image than generative baselines, providing a more direct and transparent view of the classifier's decision boundary.

</details>


### [104] [A Top-Down Framework for Metric-Scale Athlete Localization from Single Broadcast Frames](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02705)
*Thanh-Khoi Nguyen,Hoang-Phuc Nguyen,Linh-Huynh,Minh-Triet Tran*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a top-down framework for metric-scale athlete localization from single-frame broadcast footage in ultra-high-resolution imagery. The method uses Boundary-Aware Adaptive Tiling to handle extreme scale disparities and a specialized two-keypoint estimator (pelvis and ground projection) adapted from RTMPose-X to lift 2D coordinates into world coordinates via camera-calibrated ray casting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Accurate world-coordinate localization of athletes from single-frame broadcast footage is challenging due to extreme scale disparities in ultra-high-resolution imagery. Existing methods suffer from boundary-splitting artifacts and recall degradation under extreme scale variance.

Method: 1) Boundary-Aware Adaptive Tiling: A semantics-guided extension of standard sliced inference that iteratively expands tile boundaries based on coarse bounding-box predictions to ensure full object containment. 2) Adaptation of RTMPose-X architecture into a specialized two-keypoint estimator (pelvis and ground projection) with a reformulated Gated Attention Unit optimized for this geometrically coupled point pair. 3) Deterministic lifting of 2D ground projections into world coordinates via camera-calibrated ray casting.

Result: On the public test set, the method achieves a LocSim score of 97.44 and an mAP of 0.9128, outperforming the baseline by over 21%.

Conclusion: The proposed framework establishes a robust solution for high-resolution scale variance in athlete localization, effectively mitigating boundary-splitting artifacts and isolating perspective distortion as the primary source of residual localization error.

Abstract: Accurate world-coordinate localization of athletes from single-frame broadcast footage is inherently challenging due to extreme scale disparities in ultra-high-resolution imagery. In this paper, we propose a top-down framework for metric-scale athlete localization from a single calibrated frame. Our approach centers on three key contributions. First, we propose Boundary-Aware Adaptive Tiling, a semantics-guided extension of standard sliced inference. By iteratively expanding tile boundaries based on coarse bounding-box predictions, it systematically ensures full object containment, effectively mitigating boundary-splitting artifacts through a lightweight pipeline adaptation without architectural modifications. By substantially mitigating recall degradation under extreme scale variance, Boundary-Aware Adaptive Tiling enables us to isolate perspective distortion as the primary source of residual localization error. Second, we adapt the RTMPose-X architecture into a specialized two-keypoint estimator (pelvis and ground projection), employing a reformulated Gated Attention Unit optimized for this geometrically coupled point pair, and then deterministically lift the 2D ground projections into world coordinates via camera-calibrated ray casting. On the public test set, our method achieves a LocSim score of 97.44 and an mAP of 0.9128, outperforming the baseline by over 21 \% and establishing a robust solution for high-resolution scale variance.

</details>


### [105] [MV-dVRK: A Multi-Viewpoint Benchmark for Spatial Surgical Perception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02717)
*Guido Caccianiga,Sergey Prokudin,Yutong Chen,Bernard Javot,Rachael L'Orsa,Omer Burak Aladağ,Yarden Sharon,Jens Rolinger,Ivan Capobianco,Anton Deguet,Siyu Tang,Katherine J. Kuchenbecker*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MV-dVRK is the first ex-vivo surgical dataset combining multiple exposure-synchronized stereo viewpoints with accurate surface geometry and camera poses. It enables systematic comparison of zero-shot monocular, stereo, multi-stereo, and multi-view 3D reconstruction methods on surgical endoscopic images.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Large-scale training and refined optimization techniques have improved sparse multi-view 3D reconstruction, but such methods have never been rigorously evaluated on real endoscopic images. Current clinical telerobots deploy single stereo cameras, making multi-viewpoint data extremely rare. This lack of evaluation benchmark motivates the creation of MV-dVRK.

Method: The paper presents MV-dVRK, featuring: (1) Multiple exposure-synchronized stereo viewpoints, (2) Accurate surface geometry and camera poses, (3) Static subset with dense SfM reference geometry validated against industrial 3D scanner, (4) Ground-truth camera poses and sparse-view test sets. Systematic comparison of zero-shot monocular, stereo, multi-stereo, and multi-view 3D reconstruction methods with varying numbers of viewpoints.

Result: With two endoscopes, multi-stereo reconstruction achieves highest coverage. With a third viewpoint, optimization-based multi-view methods perform best, covering 67% of ground-truth surface points within 1mm tolerance and recovering highly accurate relative camera poses. Feed-forward foundation models cover only 43% in the same setting. Dataset includes ten dynamic sequences spanning multiple surgical tasks with increasing kinematic complexity and tissue deformation.

Conclusion: Optimization-based multi-view methods significantly outperform feed-forward foundation models (67% vs 43% coverage) in surgical 3D reconstruction when multiple viewpoints are available. MV-dVRK provides the first rigorous benchmark for multi-viewpoint surgical perception, enabling future research in this domain.

Abstract: Large-scale training and refined optimization techniques have greatly improved sparse multi-view 3D reconstruction. Despite their relevance to surgery, such methods have never before been rigorously evaluated on real endoscopic images. Current clinical telerobots deploy a single stereo camera inside the patient, making multi-viewpoint data extremely rare. This paper presents MV-dVRK, the first ex-vivo surgical dataset to combine multiple exposure-synchronized stereo viewpoints with accurate surface geometry and camera poses. The static subset of the benchmark provides dense SfM reference geometry, validated against an industrial 3D scanner, together with ground-truth camera poses and sparse-view test sets. We use MV-dVRK to systematically compare zero-shot monocular, stereo, multi-stereo, and multi-view 3D reconstruction methods as the number of viewpoints increases. With two endoscopes, multi-stereo reconstruction achieves the highest coverage. With a third viewpoint, optimization-based multi-view methods perform best, covering 67% of ground-truth surface points within a 1 mm tolerance and recovering highly accurate relative camera poses. By contrast, feed-forward foundation models cover only 43% of the ground-truth surface in the same setting. MV-dVRK also includes ten dynamic sequences spanning multiple surgical tasks, with increasing kinematic complexity and tissue deformation, providing a basis for future research in multi-viewpoint surgical perception. The project is available at: https://mv-dvrk.is.mpg.de.

</details>


### [106] [InceptionGS: Generative Bootstrapping for Large-Scale Gaussian Splatting under Unstructured View Sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02747)
*Tianheng Lu,Guangyu Wang,Ruqi Huang,Lu Fang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: InceptionGS is a novel method that combines Gaussian splatting with scene- and view-adaptive generative priors to handle unstructured multi-view imagery in large-scale 3D scene reconstruction. It addresses view scarcity issues by reasonably repairing problematic regions while preserving quality elsewhere.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Large-scale scene digitization faces challenges with inconsistent rendering due to unstructured sampled views, where certain regions lack sufficient observations. Existing reconstruction-based methods are vulnerable to view scarcity, while generation-based approaches suffer from generalization, controllability, and 3D consistency issues.

Method: InceptionGS bootstraps Gaussian splatting by balancing reconstruction and generation. Starting from an initial Gaussian splatting, it reasonably repairs problematic regions caused by view scarcity while preserving quality elsewhere, by softly incorporating scene- and view-adaptive generative priors.

Result: Extensive experiments on real-world large-scale scenes demonstrate the superiority and broad applicability of the approach in handling unstructured imagery and boosting high-fidelity Gaussian splatting.

Conclusion: InceptionGS successfully addresses the challenge of inconsistent rendering in large-scale scene digitization by combining reconstruction and generation approaches, offering a broadly applicable solution for handling unstructured multi-view imagery.

Abstract: Achieving truly immersive large-scale scene digitization necessitates consistent and visually pleasing rendering across all possible viewing perspectives. However, collecting multi-view images covering every fine detail of a large-scale scene is prohibitive due to scene complexity, capture cost, negligence, or accessibility constraints. As a result, the sampled views tend to be highly unstructured -- the majority of the scene is well covered yet certain regions inevitably lack sufficient observations. Existing reconstruction based methods are vulnerable to view scarcity while generation based approaches suffer from generalization, controllability, and 3D consistency issues. To address this challenge, we propose InceptionGS, which bootstraps Gaussian splatting by subtly balancing reconstruction and generation. Starting from an initial Gaussian splatting, InceptionGS reasonably rethinks and repairs problematic regions caused by view scarcity while preserving the quality elsewhere, by softly incorporating scene- and view-adaptive generative priors. Extensive experiments on real-world large-scale scenes demonstrate the superiority and broad applicability of our approach in handling unstructured imagery and boosting high-fidelity Gaussian splatting. Please refer to the supplementary video for better visual demonstrations.

</details>


### [107] [Balancing Frequencies and Pixels in Flow Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02748)
*Lucas Degeorge,Paul Couairon,Arijit Ghosh,Alexei A. Efros,David Picard,Vicky Kalogeiton*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper addresses spectral imbalance in pixel-space flow model training, where low-frequency components dominate the loss signal, delaying learning of high-frequency details (textures, edges). The authors propose a Focal Log-Frequency Loss (f-loss) that equalizes learning signal across frequencies, combined with a two-stage training strategy: frequency-domain learning first, then transition to pixel-space v-loss.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Natural images have 1/f² spectral distribution - most energy in low frequencies, while perceptually important structures (textures, edges) occupy sparse high-frequency bands. Standard pixel-space reconstruction objectives treat all spatial errors uniformly, causing low frequencies to dominate the optimization signal and delaying learning of fine-scale details. This is identified as a key inefficiency in training pixel-space flow models.

Method: 1) Focal Log-Frequency Loss (f-loss): A spectrally balanced objective that equalizes learning signal across frequencies by emphasizing high-frequency components that are otherwise underrepresented in pixel-space objectives. 2) Two-stage training strategy: First emphasize frequency-domain learning early to capture all frequencies, then transition to standard pixel-space v-loss for spatial refinement.

Result: Across multiple model scales, the approach accelerates convergence by up to 40% while consistently improving FID and perceptual fidelity. No architectural changes required - acts as a drop-in replacement for flow matching losses.

Conclusion: The proposed frequency-balanced loss and training strategy effectively addresses the low-frequency bias of pixel losses. It aligns the training signal with the evolving needs of the model, achieving significant convergence speedup and improved perceptual quality without modifying the model architecture.

Abstract: Natural images follow a $1/f^2$ spectral distribution: most signal energy lies in the low spatial frequencies, while the perceptually important structures such as textures and edges occupy sparse high-frequency bands. Pixel-space reconstruction objectives, however, treat all spatial errors uniformly, causing low frequencies to dominate the optimization signal and delaying the learning of fine-scale details. In this work, we identify this objective-level spectral imbalance as a key inefficiency in training pixel-space flow models. To address it, we propose a Focal Log-Frequency Loss (f-loss), a spectrally balanced objective that equalizes the learning signal across frequencies, emphasizing high-frequency components that are otherwise underrepresented in pixel-space objectives. Building on this, we introduce a simple training strategy that combines frequency and pixel supervision: we first emphasize frequency-domain learning early to capture all frequencies, and then transition to standard pixel-space v-loss for spatial refinement. This balancing mitigates the low-frequency bias of pixel losses and aligns the training signal with the evolving needs of the model. Our approach is conceptually simple, requires no architectural changes, and acts as a drop-in replacement for flow matching losses. Across multiple model scales, it accelerates convergence by up to 40% while consistently improving FID and perceptual fidelity. We will release code and models.

</details>


### [108] [Video-Based Palm-Vein Authentication under Challenging Conditions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02776)
*Xiaofeng Yan,Kechen Liu,Abhilash Venkatesh,Cathy Zhang,Xia Zhou,Salvatore Stolfo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper introduces the Columbia University Palm-vein (CUP) dataset, the first public video-based palm-vein dataset, to study the effects of real-world conditions on palm-vein recognition. It benchmarks 21 recognizers and proposes a robust solution combining temporal consensus and spatial matching that achieves state-of-the-art performance across all surface conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Palm-vein biometrics are increasingly used for secure, contactless authentication, but real-world deployment exposes them to various challenging conditions (surface noise, illumination/motion variation, temperature-driven changes in vascular visibility) that remain underexplored due to lack of appropriate datasets captured under such conditions.

Method: The authors create the CUP dataset with four surface conditions (clean, warm, wet, dirty) with physiological and demographic metadata. They benchmark 21 recognizers spanning static, video, and multi-frame aggregation architectures. They propose a temporal consensus approach over multiple frames to cancel transient corruption, and a test-time matcher that fuses global cosine similarity with a saliency-steered region-level optimal transport to route comparisons around corrupted regions.

Result: Models that work well on clean palms lose most accuracy on dirty ones, with mean EER roughly quadrupling. The proposed temporal-spatial approach recovers much of the robustness. The full design achieves best performance on every surface condition of CUP in EER, TAR@FAR=0.01, and Rank-1 metrics, with only 4.3M parameters and 3.1 GFLOPs. It reduces mean EER by 29-37% across four frozen state-of-the-art backbones without retraining. Demographic analysis reveals two warm-condition gaps along body water and gender that survive multiple-comparison correction.

Conclusion: The CUP dataset enables systematic study of real-world palm-vein recognition challenges. The proposed temporal-spatial fusion approach significantly improves robustness across all surface conditions while maintaining computational efficiency. The findings highlight the importance of considering environmental and physiological factors in biometric system design.

Abstract: Palm-vein biometrics are increasingly used for secure, contactless authentication. Yet real-world deployment exposes them to surface noise (sweat, dirt), illumination and motion variation, and temperature-driven changes in vascular visibility, which remain underexplored for lack of data captured under such conditions. To study these effects, we introduce the Columbia University Palm-vein (CUP) dataset, to our knowledge the first public video-based palm-vein dataset. CUP records every palm under four surface conditions (a clean baseline, warm, wet, and dirty) and pairs each subject with physiological and demographic metadata. On it we benchmark twenty-one recognizers spanning static, video, and multi-frame aggregation architectures. Models that verify reliably on clean palms lose most of their accuracy on dirty ones, and the mean equal error rate (EER) roughly quadruples. We recover much of that robustness along both axes of the capture. Temporally, a consensus over the few frames the sensor already returns cancels transient corruption; spatially, a test-time matcher that adds no learned parameters fuses the global cosine with a saliency-steered region-level optimal transport that routes the comparison around corrupted regions. The full design leads on every surface of CUP in EER, TAR@FAR=0.01, and Rank-1, at 4.3M parameters and 3.1 GFLOPs, a fraction of the video models' cost. Attached to four frozen state-of-the-art backbones it cuts their mean EER by 29-37% without retraining, and on four public single-image datasets the regional matching alone still helps. A preliminary audit across ten demographic and physiological traits finds two warm-condition gaps, along body water and gender, that survive multiple-comparison correction. CUP will be released for non-commercial research use at https://github.com/MobileX-CU/CUP_v1 upon publication.

</details>


### [109] [ShallowStream: Index Shallow then Answer Deep for Streaming Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02780)
*Jitai Hao,Ke Yang,Qiang Huang,Jun Yu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ShallowStream is a framework that uses shallow layers of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) for efficient streaming video understanding, achieving up to 52.1x reduction in per-frame prefill latency and 11.9x reduction in end-to-end latency while maintaining performance comparable to state-of-the-art methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Processing continuous video streams with MLLMs is computationally expensive due to full-depth prefill execution and KV cache growth proportional to prefill depth. Existing methods focus on token reduction but overlook model depth as an optimization dimension.

Method: ShallowStream leverages shallow MLLM layers for simultaneous frame encoding and retrieval index building. It maintains a lightweight always-on index using shallow layer KV cache. At query time, it uses attention scores to score context frames and applies a diversity-aware selection strategy to retrieve relevant evidence.

Result: ShallowStream achieves performance on par with the strongest existing streaming methods while reducing per-frame prefill latency by up to 52.1x and 10-second end-to-end latency by up to 11.9x.

Conclusion: Exploiting model depth dimension through shallow layers is a promising approach for efficient streaming video understanding, offering substantial latency reductions without sacrificing accuracy.

Abstract: Streaming video understanding is a critical capability for real-world applications, including embodied intelligence, autonomous driving, industrial monitoring, surveillance and early warning, and wearable assistants. However, processing continuous video streams with multimodal large language models (MLLMs) is computationally expensive. Existing efforts have explored reducing streaming overhead through visual token pruning, token merging, quantization, on-demand frame retrieval, and context offloading. However, most existing methods overlook the dimension of model depth. Repeatedly executing full-depth MLLM prefill over incoming frames is prohibitively expensive, incurring substantial computational overhead and causing the KV cache to grow at a rate directly proportional to the prefill depth. To address these challenges, we propose ShallowStream, a novel framework that leverages the shallow layers of an MLLM to simultaneously perform frame encoding and retrieval index building. During stream processing, ShallowStream maintains an always-on lightweight index using the KV cache of shallow layers. During query-time answering, we leverage the attention scores generated by the shallow layers to score context frames and employ a diversity-aware selection strategy to retrieve precise and comprehensive evidence. ShallowStream achieves performance on par with the strongest existing streaming methods, while reducing per-frame prefill latency and 10-second end-to-end latency by up to 52.1x and 11.9x, respectively. Our code is available at https://github.com/CURRENTF/ShallowStream.

</details>


### [110] [AutoCompass: Accurate Visual Localization on Public Maps by Learning from Weak Labels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02798)
*Javier Tirado-Garín,Alan Savio Paul,Shuai Chen,Axel Barroso-Laguna,Tommaso Cavallari,Daniyar Turmukhambetov,Victor Adrian Prisacariu,Eric Brachmann*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AutoCompass is a supervision approach for training neural map matchers (which estimate 3-DoF pose relative to 2D maps) from noisy GPS-based labels. The key insights are: (1) heading labels are unnecessary as models learn accurate headings from raw GPS automatically; (2) defining tolerance regions around raw GPS improves positional accuracy; (3) using relative poses from SLAM/SfM provides better training signal when available.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper addresses the problem that geo-referenced training datasets for neural map matchers have noisy position and heading labels, which negatively affects trained models. Current approaches heavily rely on absolute pose labels, but these contain errors that degrade model performance.

Method: The proposed AutoCompass approach has three main components: (1) Using raw GPS labels only (without heading labels) - models learn to predict accurate headings automatically; (2) Defining tolerance regions around raw GPS positions to improve positional accuracy; (3) Incorporating relative poses between training images (obtained via SLAM or SfM) as a more accurate training signal when available.

Result: AutoCompass consistently outperforms models trained with standard absolute pose labels across both driving and egocentric benchmarks.

Conclusion: The paper demonstrates that neural map matchers can be trained effectively without heading labels and that incorporating relative pose information (when available) significantly improves performance. This work challenges the conventional reliance on absolute pose labels in map matcher training.

Abstract: Neural map matchers estimate an image's 3-DoF pose relative to a 2D map. These models are trained on large-scale datasets of geo-referenced images, whose position and heading labels often contain noise that affects the trained models. To address this, we present AutoCompass, a supervision approach for training neural map matchers from inaccurate absolute pose labels. First, we show that heading labels are unnecessary: trained from raw GPS labels, models learn to predict accurate headings, automatically. Second, defining a tolerance region around raw GPS improves positional accuracy. Third, if available, our supervision uses relative poses between training images, obtained via SLAM or SfM, which provide a more accurate training signal. Across driving and egocentric benchmarks, AutoCompass consistently outperforms counterparts trained with the usual strong reliance on absolute pose labels.

</details>


### [111] [GDB-Reward: From Evaluation Metrics to Training Rewards for Graphic Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02813)
*Adrienne Deganutti,Purvanshi Mehta,Simon Hadfield,Andrew Gilbert*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GDB-Reward transforms heterogeneous graphic design evaluation metrics into unified reinforcement learning rewards for optimizing text-to-image prompts, enabling design-aware image generation without fine-tuning the frozen generator.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Text-to-image models excel at natural image synthesis but fail at graphic design tasks requiring precise constraints on typography, layout, color, and visual communication. Prompt optimization via reinforcement learning offers a solution since RL only requires scalar rewards rather than differentiable objectives.

Method: GDB-Reward framework systematically transforms heterogeneous graphic design evaluation metrics into a unified RL reward signal. This reward is used to optimize prompts for a frozen text-to-image generator, eliminating the need for expensive diffusion model fine-tuning.

Result: Substantial improvements in adherence to design specifications across perceptual quality, rendering fidelity, and spatial accuracy while keeping the image generator entirely frozen.

Conclusion: Heterogeneous, non-differentiable evaluation metrics can serve as effective optimization objectives for reinforcement learning in domains where differentiable supervision is unavailable, enabling design-aware image generation without model fine-tuning.

Abstract: Text-to-image models excel at natural image synthesis but struggle with graphic design, where success depends on satisfying precise constraints on typography, layout, color, and visual communication. While prompt optimization offers an attractive alternative to expensive diffusion model fine-tuning, learning prompts for frozen image generators requires informative reward functions despite the entirely non-differentiable generation process. Reinforcement learning does not require differentiable objectives; it requires only scalar rewards capable of ranking candidate outputs. This raises a simple question: can design evaluation metrics themselves become reinforcement learning rewards? Our central contribution is GDB-Reward, a framework that systematically transforms heterogeneous graphic design evaluation metrics into a unified reinforcement learning reward. Experiments demonstrate that GDB-Reward provides an effective optimization objective, substantially improving adherence to the design specification in perceptual quality, rendering fidelity, and spatial accuracy while keeping the image generator entirely frozen. More broadly, our results demonstrate that heterogeneous, non-differentiable evaluation metrics can move beyond passive benchmarking to become effective optimization objectives for reinforcement learning in domains where differentiable supervision is unavailable.

</details>


### [112] [Benchmarking RAW and RGB Restoration in Image Signal Processors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02831)
*Zihao Lu,Radu Timofte,Marcos V. Conde*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper benchmarks two placements for blind image restoration around a fixed ISP: pre-ISP restoration in RAW domain vs post-ISP restoration in sRGB domain. The study covers four smartphone device groups, two learned ISPs, three degradation regimes, and multiple restoration models. Key finding: placement alone doesn't determine performance - RAW restoration outperforms generic RGB models, but RGB restoration models trained with ISP-aware supervision achieve the best overall performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The research addresses a critical question in computational photography: where in the imaging pipeline should blind restoration be placed for optimal image quality? With modern cameras using ISPs to convert RAW sensor data to sRGB images, understanding the best placement strategy for restoration algorithms is essential for practical deployment.

Method: The benchmark employs a comprehensive experimental setup: (1) Four smartphone device groups representing different hardware configurations; (2) Two learned ISPs to test generalization; (3) Three degradation regimes: noise, blur, and joint noise and blur; (4) Multiple representative RAW and RGB restoration models for comparison. The study evaluates performance differences between pre-ISP (RAW domain) and post-ISP (sRGB domain) restoration placements around a fixed ISP.

Result: The results reveal that placement alone does not determine performance. RAW restoration strategies outperform the best generic RGB restoration models. However, RGB restoration models trained with consideration of ISP transformations (ISP-aware supervision) achieve the best overall performance. The study demonstrates that image reconstruction performance strongly depends on alignment between the restoration model and the target imaging pipeline.

Conclusion: The authors recommend reporting restoration placement and ISP-aware supervision as key experimental factors in future research. The performance strongly depends on alignment between restoration model and target imaging pipeline. Code is made publicly available.

Abstract: Modern cameras transform RAW sensor measurements into sRGB images through an image signal processor (ISP). We benchmark two placements for blind restoration around a fixed ISP: (A) pre-ISP restoration in the RAW domain and (B) post-ISP restoration in the sRGB domain. The benchmark covers four smartphone device groups, two learned ISPs, three degradation regimes--noise, blur, and joint noise and blur--, and several representative RAW and RGB restoration models. Our results show that placement alone does not determine performance. The RAW restoration strategy outperforms the best generic RGB restoration models. However, RGB restoration models trained considering the ISP transformations, achieve the best overall performance. Our novel benchmark demonstrates that the image reconstruction performance strongly depends on the alignment between the restoration model and the target imaging pipeline. We consequently recommend reporting restoration placement and ISP-aware supervision as key experimental factors. Our code is available at https://github.com/mv-lab/AISP

</details>


### [113] [Efficient All-in-One Weather Restoration using Spectral Harmonization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02839)
*Paula Garrido-Mellado,Daniel Feijoo,Yuning Cui,Alvaro Garcia,Marcos V. Conde*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: FReSH-IR (Frequency Reconstruction via Spectral Harmonization) is a lightweight all-in-one image restoration method that handles adverse weather conditions (rain, haze, snow) by decomposing features into high- and low-frequency components using Fourier-based skip connections in a hierarchical encoder-decoder architecture.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Adverse weather conditions significantly degrade image quality, challenging both human perception and physical AI systems. Existing restoration methods require large computational budgets and struggle to process high-resolution images while handling different types of degradations.

Method: The proposed FReSH-IR method explicitly decomposes feature representations into high- and low-frequency components at each scale of a hierarchical encoder-decoder architecture. It combines spectral decomposition with spatial processing through Fourier-based skip connections, enabling capture of complementary frequency information without sacrificing spatial detail.

Result: The approach achieves similar restoration quality with 80% fewer parameters and operations compared to transformer-based models, demonstrating an excellent efficiency-performance trade-off.

Conclusion: FReSH-IR offers a practical solution for constrained-resource systems, providing an efficient all-in-one restoration method that can handle multiple weather degradations while maintaining computational efficiency.

Abstract: Adverse weather conditions such as rain, haze, and snow significantly degrade image quality, posing challenges for both human perception and physical AI. Existing restoration methods require large computational budgets, struggling to process high-resolution images and handle different degradations. In this paper, we present Frequency Reconstruction via Spectral Harmonization, a novel lightweight all-in-one restoration method that explicitly decomposes feature representations into high- and low-frequency components at each scale of a hierarchical encoder-decoder architecture. By combining spectral decomposition with spatial processing through Fourier-based skip connections, FReSH-IR captures complementary frequency information without sacrificing spatial detail. Our approach achieves similar restoration quality with 80% fewer parameters and operations than transformer-based models. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method offers a great efficiency-performance trade-off, highlighting its practical applications in constrained-resource systems.

</details>


### [114] [RoGe: Novel View Synthesis via End-to-End Implicit Reconstruction and Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02847)
*Xiaolei Lang,Ze Kang,Zehao Huang,Naiyan Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RoGe is an end-to-end unified reconstruction and generation framework for novel view synthesis from sparse inputs. It removes the explicit bridge between reconstruction and generation used in prior methods by building an implicit scene representation from sparse views and injecting ray-queried geometric features directly into a video diffusion model as conditioning, without any 3D intermediate representations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing hybrid methods for novel view synthesis combine reconstruction and generation but rely on a bridge (rendered images or explicit 3D representations like point maps or 3D Gaussians). This approach inherits errors from the lossy reconstruction and provides no feedback loop from generation to correct reconstruction errors.

Method: RoGe builds an implicit scene representation from sparse input views using a feed-forward reconstruction model. It queries this representation with target camera rays to obtain per-view geometric features, which are then injected into a video diffusion model as conditioning without any 3D intermediate. Both the reconstruction and generation modules are trained jointly, allowing the generation objective to directly shape its own geometric conditioning.

Result: On DL3DV, RoGe outperforms reconstruction-based, generation-based, and hybrid baselines on image-level metrics and video-level temporal consistency. Ablations confirm that ray-queried implicit features outperform both raw reconstruction tokens and rendered RGB as conditioning, and that joint training brings further gains.

Conclusion: The proposed approach demonstrates that removing the explicit bridge between reconstruction and generation, using ray-queried implicit features as conditioning for video diffusion, achieves superior results. The joint training of both modules is crucial for optimal performance, as it allows the generation objective to directly optimize the geometric conditioning it receives.

Abstract: Novel view synthesis from sparse inputs requires both geometric grounding from the observed views and generative priors of unobserved regions, motivating recent hybrid methods that combine reconstruction and generation. However, existing methods bridge the two with rendered images or explicit 3D representations such as point maps or 3D Gaussians. Generation is thus conditioned on a lossy and imperfect projection of the scene, inheriting its errors, and reconstruction receives no signal from generation to correct them. We present RoGe, an end-to-end unified reconstruction and generation framework that removes this explicit bridge. It targets roaming within a scene anchored by sparse views: given a few posed images and a camera trajectory, it synthesizes a temporally coherent video along that trajectory. From the sparse input views, RoGe builds an implicit scene representation with a feed-forward reconstruction model, and queries it with target camera rays to obtain per-view geometric features. These features are injected into a video diffusion model as conditioning, without any 3D intermediate. Both modules are trained jointly, so the generation objective directly shapes its own geometric conditioning. We conduct experiments on DL3DV, where RoGe outperforms reconstruction-based, generation-based, and hybrid baselines on image-level metrics and video-level temporal consistency. Ablations confirm that ray-queried implicit features outperform both raw reconstruction tokens and rendered RGB as conditioning, and that joint training brings further gains.

</details>


### [115] [MuyBridge: Mobile Human Center-of-Mass Estimation from Monocular Video via Sparse Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02854)
*Aidan Bradshaw,Marco Giordano,David Rode,Andreas Habersack,Elif Basokur,Annika Kruse,Markus Tilp,Michele Magno,Peter Wolf,Luca Benini,Christoph Leitner*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MuyBridge is an on-device system that estimates 3D center of mass (CoM) trajectory from a single phone camera video, using a compact 2D pose network coupled with a monocular depth network through analytic metric fusion with anatomical and physical priors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The 3D center of mass is crucial for biomechanical analysis in sports, rehabilitation, and clinical movement, but existing methods either lack anatomical constraints or require heavy compute infrastructure, making it difficult for coaches and movement analysts to measure CoM from a single camera where athletes train and compete.

Method: MuyBridge combines a compact 2D pose network with a distilled single-step monocular depth network through analytic metric fusion that uses anatomical and physical priors to anchor the metric CoM, requiring no 3D or task-specific supervision.

Result: Evaluated on athletic movements (running, track and field, figure skating) from AthletePose3D, MuyBridge achieves 33-41 mm vertical CoM error and 2.3-6.6% absolute-relative range error (AbsRel) under one-time calibration, running at 63 FPS pose-estimation rate with asynchronous 2.86 Hz depth updates on iPhone 15.

Conclusion: MuyBridge enables practical, on-device CoM tracking from a single phone camera, providing accurate metric CoM estimates without requiring 3D or task-specific supervision, making itdeployable in real-world training and competition environments.

Abstract: The 3D center of mass (CoM) is a primary quantity in the biomechanical analysis of sport, rehabilitation, and clinical movement, yet existing 3D pose tracking, mesh recovery, and multi-view triangulation methods either optimize 3D keypoint accuracy without anatomical constraints or carry compute and capture infrastructure too heavy to deploy where CoM tracking is most useful. As a result, the metric CoM remains difficult for coaches and movement analysts to measure from a single camera where athletes train and compete. In this work, we introduce MuyBridge, an on-device system that estimates the athlete's segmental center of mass trajectory from a single phone camera video stream. MuyBridge couples a compact 2D pose network and a distilled single-step monocular depth network through an analytic metric fusion that uses anatomical and physical priors to anchor the metric CoM, requiring no 3D or task-specific supervision. Evaluated on the athletic movements of AthletePose3D (running, track and field, and figure skating), MuyBridge achieves 33-41 mm vertical CoM error and 2.3-6.6% absolute-relative range error (AbsRel) under a one-time calibration, and produces CoM estimates at the 63 FPS pose-estimation rate using asynchronous 2.86 Hz depth updates on iPhone 15. Code is available at: https://github.com/Abradshaw1/Muybridge

</details>


### [116] [PlantC2USeg: Cross-Scale Consistent Pre-Training for Few-Shot Unified Plant Point Cloud Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02860)
*Yu Tian,Xintong Jiang,Jan Franklin Adamowski,Shiv O. Prasher,Shangpeng Sun*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: PlantC2USeg is a deep transfer learning framework for plant point cloud segmentation that addresses the challenge of requiring densely annotated datasets. It uses cross-scale consistency learning to align features across spatial scales and an information-restricted decoding strategy to prevent reconstruction shortcuts. The framework enables few-shot generalization across species and sensing conditions with minimal labeled data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Modern crop breeding requires precise organ-level analysis for trait quantification, making plant point cloud segmentation (PPCS) increasingly important. However, conventional deep learning approaches rely heavily on densely annotated datasets that are labor-intensive to acquire. Unified PPCS adaptation from distribution-shifted examples with minimal additional training remains challenging.

Method: PlantC2USeg features: (1) Cross-scale consistency learning to explicitly align features across spatial scales, (2) Information-restricted decoding strategy that prevents reconstruction shortcuts and promotes robust adaptation, (3) Pre-training enabling stable few-shot generalization across species and sensing conditions, (4) Unified fine-tuning with inherited thresholds to reduce adaptation overhead.

Result: - Soybean3D (full supervision): 91.91% semantic IoU, 94.62% instance mWCov
